{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_10","type":"command_execution","command":"\"C:\\\\Program Files\\\\PowerShell\\\\7\\\\pwsh.exe\" -Command 'Get-Content -Raw \"prompts/runs/20260308-171858-core-engine-fec-f3e-deep-research-pack-01/inputs/source/foundation-core__ARCHITECTURE_AND_REQUIREMENTS.md\"'","aggregated_output":"# ARCHITECTURE_AND_REQUIREMENTS.md — DNA Calc Architecture and Requirements\r\n\r\n## 1. Overview\r\nDNA Calc is a near-formal spreadsheet system with two independent engines (Rust and .NET) sharing identical protocol surfaces and validated against a Green-owned spec stack: Lean (semantics proofs), TLA+ (concurrency protocol checks), OCaml oracle (executable reference), and conformance packs.\r\n\r\n### 1.1 Three Hard Boundaries (core architectural shape)\r\n1. **OpLog (Operations)**\r\n   - All persistent state changes are operations (including structural edits, external updates, macro edits).\r\n2. **DocSnapshot (Versioned Document State)**\r\n   - Immutable snapshots per epoch/meta-epoch. Inputs are truth; derived values are caches.\r\n3. **CalcDeltas (Derived Outputs)**\r\n   - Engine produces deltas tagged with version info (epoch/value_epoch) and explicit stale/pending status.\r\n\r\n## 2. Requirements Taxonomy (how to write requirements)\r\n### 2.1 Architecture-independent requirements (REQ-)\r\nObservable behaviors and quality targets, independent of internal mechanisms.\r\n\r\n### 2.2 Architecture-dependent constraints (CONSTR-)\r\nEnforceable structural rules derived from Mission/Doctrine.\r\n\r\n### 2.3 Architecture-anchored intents and realizations (INT-/REAL-)\r\n- **Intent (INT-)**: desired outcome, mechanism-agnostic.\r\n- **Realization (REAL-)**: precise, testable specification anchored to chosen architecture.\r\n\r\n## 3. System Architecture (A1)\nThis section now includes a formal-core layer model intended to be shared by Green proofs/models, the OCaml oracle, and both delivery engines.\nDetailed core formal/semantic model development is maintained in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md`.\nThis document should keep architecture-level summaries and stable constraints, with minimal duplication of formal-core detail.\n\n### 3.1 Protocol Surface (identical across Red/Blue)\n- Dispatch ops/transactions.\n- Query snapshots/state.\n- Subscribe to deltas/events.\n- Capability negotiation.\n\nDetailed core semantics are in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md`:\n- protocol baseline: Section `5.1`,\n- operation/replay semantics: Section `6.4`.\n\n### 3.2 Profiles, Feature Gates, and Compatibility\n- Profiles remain the semantic spine (`profile_id` + `profile_version`) for compatibility, feature gates, obligation packs, and degrade behavior.\n\nDetailed core profile semantics are in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` Section `5.2`.\n\n### 3.3 Epoch Model (MVCC-style)\n- Core epoch semantics (`committed/stabilized/value epoch`, stale/pending visibility, pinning/GC constraints) remain mandatory.\n- Core calc-delta derived-output semantics remain mandatory.\n\nDetailed definitions are in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` Section `5.3`.\n\n### 3.4 Calculation Engine Pipeline (conceptual)\n- Core pipeline remains parse/bind/dependency/invalidation/schedule/evaluate/commit with deterministic and incremental requirements.\n- `NodeId`-based dependency identity remains baseline.\n- Both invariant-oriented and dirty-closure propagation models remain valid formal baselines.\n\nDetailed definitions are in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` Section `5.4`.\n\n### 3.5 External Streaming and RTD-like Behavior\n- Pathfinder: `STREAM(\"topic\")` is acceptable and deterministic (epoch-scoped external provider).\n- Full system: RTD support (topic lifecycle, updates, invalidations) is a core interop feature.\n- External updates must appear as explicit `OpExternalUpdate` ops (`topic_id`, `topic_seq`, payload ref/envelope) and be replayable for test harnesses where required.\n- STREAM/external update semantics include explicit topic identity, dedupe rules, ordering guarantees, and coalescing policy.\n- Profile policy defines whether oracle values are local-only or shared for collaboration scenarios.\n- A stream replay bundle (topic declarations, updates, timing/order envelope) is a required artifact for conformance and minimization.\n\n#### 3.5.1 External Invalidation vs Volatile Invalidation\n- External functions (`STREAM`, RTD, and profile-marked externally-invalidated UDFs) recalculate on explicit external signal, not on every volatile cycle.\n- Volatile functions recalculate on invalidation cycles triggered by host policy.\n- Distinct invalidation pathways are required:\n  - volatile invalidation scope (global or class-based),\n  - external invalidation scope (targeted by provider/topic/UDF identity).\n- Both pathways converge on the same dirty-closure propagation and deterministic evaluation pipeline.\n\n### 3.6 External UDFs / XLL-like integration\nPathfinder scope includes:\n- external UDF registration/unregistration with explicit volatility class,\n- externally driven invalidation hooks (`invalidate_udf`) that feed normal dirty-closure propagation,\n- deterministic caller-driven execution (no autonomous internal timers/threads in pathfinder engine boundary),\n- UDFs treated as pure-oracle from Lean/TLA+ perspective (semantics parameterized by oracle results).\n\nVolatility classification:\n- `Standard`: recalculates when upstream dependencies change.\n- `Volatile`: recalculates on host invalidation cycle.\n- `ExternallyInvalidated`: recalculates on explicit external signal.\n\nBuilt-in and UDF volatility classification is profile-governed and must be reflected in capability/profile artifacts.\n\nFull system adds:\n- full XLL compatibility including marshalling/lifetime contracts and RTD integration.\n- XLL is in-process with the engine; boundary contracts are formally specified and validated by Green packs.\n\n### 3.6.1 Controls and Charts as Engine Entities\n- Controls/charts are core engine entities participating in dependency semantics and operation/replay lifecycle.\n\nDetailed core semantics are in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` Section `5.6`.\n\r\n### 3.7 VBA and Macros (outside core)\r\n- VBA runtime and editor live outside the core engine.\r\n- Core engine stores the VBA project as a document object:\r\n  - opaque blob (e.g., `vbaProject.bin`) + minimal metadata.\r\n- Application layer glues:\r\n  - file I/O ↔ engine (store/retrieve blob),\r\n  - VBA runtime ↔ engine (macros emit ops via protocol),\r\n  - macro execution occurs in an exclusive mutation mode (serialized event stream).\r\n\r\nWindows-only COM automation is a separate facade layered on top of the identical protocol surface.\r\n\r\n### 3.8 File I/O (outside core, full fidelity)\n- File adapters are external components that translate to/from the object model and ops.\n- For Excel interop:\n  - preserve unknown/unsupported OOXML parts where feasible (opaque attachments),\n  - never silently drop meaning on round-trip,\n  - lowering pipeline may translate internal constructs to Excel-safe constructs or explicit loss markers,\n  - degrade outcomes must be surfaced through diagnostics with policy class (`Native` / `Lowered` / `Opaque` / `Rejected`).\n\r\n### 3.9 Collaboration (designed-in seam)\n- Collaboration modeled as replication of the OpLog.\n- Initial design preference: server-sequenced ops (deterministic shared log).\n- Identity under structural edits is considered early (stable IDs where needed).\n- Derived calc is generally local; external oracles (RTD) may be local or shared depending on profile policy.\n- Replication envelope requires operation idempotency, causal ordering metadata, and transaction grouping.\n\n### 3.10 UI Architecture (intended stack)\n- Tauri shell with web UI.\n- Grid rendering:\n  - Canvas/WebGL for the giant grid (virtualized; no DOM-per-cell).\n  - DOM overlay editor for Excel-grade editing (IME, selection, clipboard).\n- UI state machine (“reducer” style) with explicit modes (selecting, editing, formula ref picking, fill, resize).\n- Geometry spec and hit-test invariants.\n- RenderPlan IR used for deterministic testing (avoid screenshot brittleness).\n- View state is partially document-backed (saved view settings) and partially session state.\n- UI reliability requires property-level invariants for geometry/hit-test consistency and deterministic RenderPlan validation.\n\n### 3.10.1 Core and Non-core Interaction Summary\n- UI/TUI layers read snapshots/deltas and dispatch explicit operations; no hidden persistent core mutation path is allowed.\n- File adapters translate external formats to/from core entities and operations, and surface degradation outcomes explicitly.\n- VBA/macro runtimes execute outside core and interact through operation pathways.\n- Collaboration adapters replicate operation envelopes and preserve causality/idempotency constraints.\n\nFormal-model note:\n- Detailed formal semantics for the core engine model are in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md`.\n- Sections `3.11`..`3.17` in this document are architecture summaries linked to that detailed model.\n\n### 3.11 Formal State Kernel (tree-grid hybrid with persistence facades)\nDetailed model is in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` (`6.1`).\nArchitecture summary:\n- immutable green core + ephemeral red facade split,\n- identity is ID-based (not coordinate-string-based),\n- representation strategy may vary while preserving identical semantics.\n\n### 3.12 Layered Semantics (structure, refs, deps, values, ops)\nDetailed model is in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` (`6.2`).\nArchitecture summary:\n- five-layer model `S/R/D/V/O`,\n- explicit derivation contracts between layers,\n- mutation authority remains operation-driven.\n\n### 3.13 OpLog Formal Transition Semantics\nDetailed model is in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` (`6.4`).\nArchitecture summary:\n- `OpEnvelope` is the canonical persistent-change carrier,\n- `apply_op` is the canonical transition relation,\n- replay target is observational equivalence under profile constraints.\n\n### 3.14 Structural Rewrite Semantics (rows/cols/sheets)\nDetailed model is in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` (`6.5`).\nArchitecture summary:\n- structural edits require deterministic rewrite functions and reference-classification outputs,\n- invalidated targets must remain explicit and traceable.\n\n### 3.15 Reference Resolution and Reference-Grid Update Semantics\nDetailed model is in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` (`6.3`).\nArchitecture summary:\n- normalized reference forms are required for deterministic binder outputs,\n- reference-grid updates must preserve explicit forward/reverse/provenance information,\n- unresolved references remain explicit errors, never silent drops.\n\n### 3.16 Cycle Detection, Iteration, and Stabilization Semantics\nDetailed model is in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` (`6.6`).\nArchitecture summary:\n- SCC processing order is deterministic,\n- cycle mode is profile-governed (`CycleError` vs `Iterative`),\n- stabilization rules are explicit and terminal-state based.\n\n### 3.17 Formalization Seams for Lean and OCaml\nDetailed seam definitions are in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` (`6.7`).\nArchitecture summary:\n- Lean, OCaml oracle, and shared trace schemas remain mandatory integration seams,\n- the `CoreIds/CoreStructure/CoreRefs/CoreDeps/CoreEval/CoreOps` split remains baseline.\n\n### 3.18 FEC/F3E Lane Boundary (OxFml/OxFunc/FEC)\nTo reduce ownership drift during implementation spikes, lane ownership is explicitly split as follows:\n- **OxFml** owns formula grammar, parse, and bind semantics.\n- **OxFunc** owns value-type and function semantics consumed during evaluation.\n- **FEC host/model lane** owns host protocol, capability policy, dependency lifecycle, scheduler interaction, and publication lifecycle.\n\nCurrent working references:\n- `reference/conformance/excel-worksheet-engine/model/EXCEL_FORMULA_EVALUATION_CONTEXT_FEC.md`\n- `reference/conformance/excel-worksheet-engine/model/FEC_F3E_INTERFACE_DRAFT_SPEC.md`\n- `reference/conformance/excel-worksheet-engine/model/FEC_F3E_PROTOCOL_CONFORMANCE_MATRIX.csv`\n\n## 4. Architectural Constraints (A2 / CONSTR- examples)\n- **CONSTR-001:** All persistent mutations are ops; direct document mutation is forbidden outside the coordinator.\n- **CONSTR-002:** File and network I/O are adapters outside core; core engine has no socket/file dependencies.\n- **CONSTR-003:** Unsupported constructs never crash; they preserve or degrade explicitly.\n- **CONSTR-004:** Protocol surfaces are identical across Red/Blue; compatibility negotiation is mandatory.\n- **CONSTR-005:** Deterministic mode must exist and be used for conformance and minimization runs.\n- **CONSTR-006:** Spec stack/oracle/tool integration contracts are file/CLI-based with schema-versioned artifacts.\n- **CONSTR-007:** Compatibility claims require linked clean-room evidence records tied to REQ/INT/REAL identifiers.\n- **CONSTR-008:** Engine lock discipline forbids awaiting or user-callback execution while holding mutation-critical locks.\n- **CONSTR-009:** Performance readiness uses deterministic phase counters and published scaling signatures per required profile.\n- **CONSTR-010:** Snapshot identity is ID-based (`RowId`/`ColId`/`CellId`), not coordinate-string-based; address projection is derived.\n- **CONSTR-011:** Every structural op must define deterministic axis rewrite functions and explicit rewrite classification for affected references.\n- **CONSTR-012:** Reference layer must model region nodes and error references explicitly; unresolved references cannot be silently discarded.\n- **CONSTR-013:** Cycle handling mode (`CycleError` or `Iterative`) is profile-defined and deterministic with explicit terminal behavior.\n- **CONSTR-014:** Op envelopes require idempotency/causality metadata sufficient for deterministic replay and replication safety.\n- **CONSTR-015:** Green/Red persistence-facade split must preserve immutable core semantics and avoid hidden mutation in facade caches.\n- **CONSTR-016:** Function invalidation classes (`Standard` / `Volatile` / `ExternallyInvalidated`) must have deterministic, non-ambiguous trigger semantics per profile.\n- **CONSTR-017:** Control/chart lifecycle mutations must be represented as explicit operations and replay artifacts, never as UI-only hidden state.\n\n## 5. Core Requirements (REQ- and INT-/REAL- examples)\n### REQ (architecture-independent)\n- Excel interop: load/save macro-enabled workbooks with no unexpected loss; preserve VBA project unless explicitly edited.\n- Manual and auto recalc behaviors must match the profile definition.\r\n- Streaming updates propagate to dependents; system exposes progress and staleness.\n- UI remains responsive under defined workloads (scrolling/edit feedback targets per profile).\n- System never crashes on unsupported features; must yield deterministic errors/warnings or preserve opaque.\n- Structural edits must preserve or invalidate references deterministically with explicit diagnostics and replayable rewrite traces.\n- OpLog replay of an accepted operation sequence must reproduce equivalent snapshot/value states across engines.\n- Cycle behavior (error or iterative) must be observable, deterministic, and profile-consistent.\n- Reference-grid updates must be incrementally maintained and auditable after every structural or formula mutation.\n- CalcDelta outputs must be epoch-tagged, typed, and observationally consistent with committed snapshot transitions.\n\n### INT/REAL (architecture-anchored)\n- **INT:** Users can trust what they see during recalculation.  \n  **REAL:** Every value carries `value_epoch` and explicit stale/pending status in UI/API.\n- **INT:** Custom features must not break other builds.  \n  **REAL:** Unknown extension payloads round-trip; unsupported semantic extensions evaluate to explicit deterministic errors and emit diagnostics.\n- **INT:** STREAM/external updates must be predictable and replayable across engines.  \n  **REAL:** External updates are explicit OpLog operations with versioned stream semantics, deterministic replay bundles, and pack-validated ordering/dedupe behavior.\n- **INT:** Volatile and externally-signaled recalculation must not be conflated.  \n  **REAL:** Profiles classify functions as `Standard` / `Volatile` / `ExternallyInvalidated` with explicit invalidation triggers and deterministic dirty-scope behavior.\n- **INT:** UI correctness must be testable without screenshot dependence.  \n  **REAL:** Geometry/hit-test invariants and RenderPlan determinism are required and pack-gated.\n- **INT:** Performance claims must be trend-checkable, not anecdotal.  \n  **REAL:** Required profiles publish deterministic phase counters and slope-based scaling signatures with regression thresholds.\n- **INT:** Clean-room compatibility claims must be auditable.  \n  **REAL:** Every compatibility claim links to admissible evidence records and review status.\n- **INT:** Structural change semantics must be predictable and formally checkable.  \n  **REAL:** Structural ops produce deterministic axis rewrite mappings plus per-reference rewrite classification artifacts.\n- **INT:** Reference resolution ambiguity must be bounded and diagnosable.  \n  **REAL:** Binder outputs normalized references (`CellRef`/`RegionRef`/`NameRef`/`ErrorRef`) and explicit unresolved diagnostics.\n- **INT:** The formal core must be implementable consistently in Lean, OCaml, Rust, and .NET.  \n  **REAL:** Shared algebraic data schemas and transition traces are normative artifacts for proofs, oracle runs, and engine conformance.\n- **INT:** Cycles should not produce hidden nondeterminism.  \n  **REAL:** SCC decomposition order, iteration bounds, convergence policy, and terminal-state rules are profile-governed and replayable.\n\n## 6. Pathfinder Scope Anchor (DnaVisiCalc)\nRound 0 pathfinder functional scope is authoritative in the DnaVisiCalc docs set:\n- `..\\\\DnaVisiCalc\\\\docs\\\\SPEC_v0.md`\n- `..\\\\DnaVisiCalc\\\\docs\\\\ENGINE_REQUIREMENTS.md`\n- `..\\\\DnaVisiCalc\\\\docs\\\\ENGINE_API.md`\n\nFoundation documents define doctrine/architecture/process and must remain consistent with that authoritative functional scope.\n\n### 6.1 Round 0 Normative Contract (minimum)\n- Required functional scope baseline (frozen for pathfinder v0) includes:\n  - externally driven single-sheet engine contract with default `A1..BK254` bounds,\n  - cell/name inputs, deterministic formula evaluation, dynamic arrays/spill behavior,\n  - epoch model (`committed_epoch`, `stabilized_epoch`, `value_epoch`) and manual/auto recalc,\n  - TUI interaction/testing scope (editing/commands plus deterministic replay/capture surfaces) as defined by upstream pathfinder docs.\n- Required obligations: core semantics packs, epoch/concurrency invariants, oracle alignment, and basic scaling signature.\n- Required artifacts: capability manifest, conformance report, minimized trace corpus, replay bundles for stream cases, and formal-core traces (structural rewrite + reference-grid delta + SCC iteration).\n\n### 6.2 Explicit Non-goals for Round 0\n- Multi-sheet workbook semantics.\n- Full number-format mini-language and full date/time serial compatibility system.\n- Full Excel coercion parity and implicit-intersection (`@`) compatibility breadth.\n- Lambda-helper family completeness beyond the stabilized pathfinder subset.\n- Full XLL marshalling/lifetime compatibility.\n- Full RTD lifecycle parity.\n- Full OOXML fidelity breadth outside the pathfinder subset.\n- VBA runtime hosting.\n- Multi-writer collaboration semantics beyond seam validation.\n\n### 6.3 Round 0 Track Status Decomposition (Pathfinder Feedback Snapshot)\nAs of **February 27, 2026**, synthesis of DnaVisiCalc pathfinder feedback indicates:\n\n- Track A — Engine implementation scope:\n  - status: design/API artifacts exist and should be treated as evidence inputs for Round 1 shaping, not as Round 0 gate substitutes.\n\nRound 0 exit remains blocked until required Track B obligations are completed, regardless of Track A progress.\n\n### 6.4 Deferred Functional Expansion Backlog (Retained From Pathfinder Gap Analysis)\nThe following areas are retained as explicit post-freeze expansion candidates, not pathfinder-v0 functional-scope requirements:\n- comprehensive number-format code language behavior,\n- full date/time serial compatibility system,\n- full coercion-matrix parity and implicit-intersection behavior,\n- multi-sheet references and workbook semantics,\n- broader lambda-helper family coverage.\n\n## 7. Purpose and Status\nThis document is the consolidated story-so-far for DNA Calc core engine formal/semantic modeling.\n\nIt is not:\n- the pathfinder functional-scope source (that remains in DnaVisiCalc docs),\n- the final formal model design,\n- the final theorem/spec artifact set.\n\nIt is:\n- the single active funnel for core-engine formal ideas,\n- a triaged and tagged consolidation of what we currently know, suspect, and need to decide,\n- the working base for iterative review and promotion into stable formal semantics over time.\n\n## 2. Source Corpus and Context\nCore context docs used in this consolidation:\n- `README.md`\n- `CHARTER.md`\n- `ARCHITECTURE_AND_REQUIREMENTS.md`\n- `OPERATIONS.md`\n- `notes/BRAINSTORM_NOTES.md`\n\nArchived formal-idea sources consolidated here:\n- `notes/archive/formal-model/FORMAL_MODELS_IDEAS.md`\n- `notes/archive/formal-model/FORMAL_MODEL_REMAINING_NOTES.md`\n- `notes/archive/formal-model/FORMAL_CORE_STATUS_AND_SUGGESTIONS_DRAFT.md`\n\n## 3. Triage Tags and Promotion Rules\nThis document uses explicit tags on ideas and decisions:\n- `Baseline`: intended current core model baseline.\n- `Provisional`: accepted direction with unresolved semantics.\n- `Exploratory`: research/design-space material not yet normative.\n- `Deferred`: explicit backlog item retained for later formalization.\n- `Out-of-core`: important system concern, but not part of the core-engine formal model.\n\nPromotion from `Exploratory`/`Provisional` to `Baseline` requires:\n- a concrete semantic decision need (profile/pack/replay impact),\n- deterministic artifact/test expression,\n- doctrine consistency,\n- synthesis decision logging.\n\n## 4. Core Boundary for This Document\nCore engine scope captured here includes:\n- protocol-facing core semantics (`dispatch/query/subscribe/capability` behavior shape),\n- profile and compatibility semantics as they constrain core behavior,\n- epoch/status model and calc-delta model,\n- dependency/recalc semantics (including cycles/iteration),\n- operation transition/replay semantics,\n- structural rewrite and reference model semantics,\n- core entities participating in dependency semantics (cells, names, charts, controls as modeled source/sink nodes).\n\n`Out-of-core` system areas are tracked only as interface/seam constraints:\n- VBA runtime hosting/editor,\n- full file adapters and OOXML lowering,\n- UI rendering architecture,\n- collaboration envelope implementation details.\n\n## 5. Consolidated Core Semantics Baseline\n\n### 5.1 Protocol Surface and Contracts (`Baseline`)\nProtocol surface (identical across implementations):\n- dispatch ops/transactions,\n- query snapshots/state,\n- subscribe to deltas/events,\n- capability negotiation.\n\nContract requirements:\n- versioned protocol surface,\n- schema-defined payloads,\n- deterministic replay-compatible semantics.\n\n### 5.2 Profiles, Gates, and Compatibility (`Baseline`)\nProfiles are semantic control planes for core behavior:\n- bind behavior to `profile_id` + `profile_version`,\n- define semantics and structural-edit rules,\n- define required obligation packs,\n- define degradation classes (`Native` / `Lowered` / `Opaque` / `Rejected`),\n- define feature-gate tokens (for example stream semantics and external update policies).\n\n### 5.3 Epoch and CalcDelta Semantics (`Baseline`)\nEpoch model:\n- `committed_epoch`: latest accepted changes,\n- `stabilized_epoch`: latest epoch with stabilized derived outputs,\n- `value_epoch`: per-derived-value epoch stamp.\n\nStatus/invariant model:\n- explicit stale/pending/ready/error visibility,\n- no stale commit (`value must match claimed input epoch`),\n- structural commit exclusivity,\n- pinned-epoch-safe GC behavior.\n\nCalcDelta model (derived output, not mutation log):\n```text\nChangeEntryKind =\n  | CellValue\n  | NameValue\n  | ChartOutput\n  | SpillRegion\n  | CellFormat\n```\nRequired properties:\n- epoch tagging,\n- deterministic emission policy,\n- drain-style retrieval contract accepted as baseline.\n\n### 5.4 Calculation Semantics (`Baseline`)\nPipeline semantics:\n- parse -> bind -> dependency graph -> invalidation closure -> schedule -> evaluate -> commit.\n\nIdentity model for evaluation graph:\n```text\nNodeId =\n  | Cell(CellId)\n  | Name(NameId)\n  | Chart(ChartId)\n```\n\nIncrementality:\n- invariant-oriented model (`necessary`, `stale`, `height`, `scope`) retained,\n- baseline dirty-closure model retained:\n  - `dirty_nodes`,\n  - `reverse_deps`,\n  - deterministic dirty-subgraph evaluation.\n\nDeterminism requirements:\n- stable scheduling behavior,\n- explicit numeric reduction policy where relevant,\n- persisted calc order artifacts treated as cache/diagnostic, not semantic truth.\n\n### 5.5 External Invalidation and UDF Semantics (`Baseline` + `Provisional`)\nThree invalidation classes:\n- `Standard`: upstream-dependent invalidation,\n- `Volatile`: host-cycle invalidation,\n- `ExternallyInvalidated`: explicit external signal invalidation.\n\nExternal stream/update semantics retained:\n- explicit external-update operation representation (`OpExternalUpdate`-style semantics),\n- explicit topic identity and sequence handling,\n- deterministic ordering/dedupe/coalescing policy by profile,\n- replay-bundle expectations for conformance/minimization flows.\n\nRequired split:\n- volatile invalidation path and external invalidation path are distinct,\n- both converge through deterministic dirty-closure/evaluation pipeline.\n\nUDF baseline:\n- registration/unregistration,\n- volatility-class declaration,\n- externally driven invalidation hooks,\n- caller-driven deterministic execution contract.\n\n`Provisional`/open:\n- deeper pure/impure function-class formal treatment for dependency-discovery interaction.\n\n### 5.6 Controls and Charts as Core Entities (`Baseline`)\nControls/charts are core engine entities (not UI-only state):\n- controls as named-value source entities with metadata/constraints,\n- charts as sink entities producing structured outputs,\n- both participate in dependency semantics and dirty propagation,\n- lifecycle changes flow through explicit operation kinds and replay artifacts.\n\n## 6. Core Formal State Kernel and Transition Semantics\n\n### 6.1 Tree-Grid Hybrid Kernel (`Baseline`)\nPersistent model shape:\n- immutable green core,\n- ephemeral red context facades,\n- ID-based identity (`RowId`/`ColId`/`CellId`), coordinate projection derived.\n\nCanonical identity sketch:\n```text\ntype Epoch = u64\ntype WorkbookId = opaque\ntype SheetId = opaque\ntype RowId = opaque\ntype ColId = opaque\ntype CellId = RowId * ColId\n```\n\nTree-grid hybrid concept retained from earlier notes:\n- workbook/sheet/grid structural substrate,\n- no arbitrary unrooted calc-node bags,\n- augmentation structures may attach and participate via defined seams.\n\n### 6.2 Layered Formal Model (`Baseline`)\n```text\nLayer S: Structure\nLayer R: References\nLayer D: Dependencies\nLayer V: Values/iteration state\nCross-cutting O: Operations\n```\n\nContracts:\n- `R` derives from `S` + bind context,\n- `D` derives from `R`,\n- `V` commit validity is epoch-strict,\n- `O` is exclusive persistent mutation pathway.\n\n### 6.3 Reference Model (`Provisional`)\nBinding context includes workbook/sheet/anchor/address-mode/profile semantics.\n\nNormalized references:\n```text\nBoundRef =\n  | CellRef(CellId)\n  | RegionRef(SheetId, RowIdRange, ColIdRange)\n  | NameRef(NameId, resolved_target?)\n  | ExternalRef(ProviderId, TopicId)\n  | ErrorRef(ErrorKind, origin_span)\n```\n\nInvariants:\n- explicit forward/reverse dependency indices,\n- explicit unresolved/error references,\n- structural edit rewrite provenance retained.\n\nOpen hotspot:\n- `RegionRef` canonical domain under structural rewrites.\n\n### 6.4 Operations and Replay Model (`Baseline` + `Provisional`)\nCanonical persistent-change envelope:\n```text\nOpEnvelope = {\n  op_id,\n  tx_id?,\n  actor_id,\n  base_epoch,\n  profile_id,\n  profile_version,\n  op_kind,\n  payload,\n  causality,\n  wall_clock_utc\n}\n```\n\nTransition relation:\n```text\napply_op(profile, snapshot_e, op) -> Result(snapshot_e_plus_1, OpError)\n```\n\nRequired transition phases:\n1. envelope/schema/idempotency/causality validation,\n2. layer mutation by op kind,\n3. reference rebind/normalize,\n4. dependency closure rebuild (affected scope),\n5. dirty/pending marking,\n6. deterministic calc/delta emission,\n7. snapshot publish.\n\nReplay equivalence:\n- `Baseline`: observational equivalence under same profile,\n- `Provisional`: stronger equality targets (structural/id-preserving/serialization).\n\n### 6.5 Structural Rewrite Model (`Baseline`)\nRequired rewrite functions:\n```text\nmu_row : RowIndex_old -> RowIndex_new | Invalid\nmu_col : ColIndex_old -> ColIndex_new | Invalid\n```\n\nRequired per-reference outcomes:\n- `Preserved`, `Shifted`, `Expanded`, `Contracted`, `Invalidated`.\n\nRequired behavior:\n- rewrite semantics are deterministic,\n- invalidated references persist explicitly,\n- rewrite traces are deterministic artifacts.\n\n### 6.6 Cycles and Iteration (`Baseline` + `Deferred`)\nCycle processing baseline:\n- SCC decomposition in stable order,\n- acyclic SCC topological evaluation,\n- cyclic SCC profile mode (`CycleError` or `Iterative`).\n\nIteration baseline:\n- bounded policy,\n- explicit progress states,\n- deterministic terminal behavior on non-convergence.\n\n`Deferred` details:\n- default bounds/tolerance/rounding policy lock.\n\n### 6.7 Formalization Seams (`Baseline`)\nSeam artifacts:\n- Lean core data types and theorem targets,\n- OCaml oracle executable transition model,\n- shared deterministic trace schemas:\n  - operation trace,\n  - structural rewrite trace,\n  - reference-grid delta trace,\n  - SCC iteration trace,\n  - value-commit trace.\n\nBaseline module split:\n- `CoreIds`, `CoreStructure`, `CoreRefs`, `CoreDeps`, `CoreEval`, `CoreOps`.\n\n## 7. Consolidated Idea Funnel (No-Loss Triage)\n\n### 7.1 Captured High-Value Core Ideas (`Exploratory` unless noted)\nFrom archived formal ideas and brainstorm sources, retained here:\n- 1+4D substrate framing (process/workbook/worksheet/grid) (`Exploratory framing`, `Baseline-compatible`).\n- augmentation-tree participation model and uniform layering mechanism (`Provisional`).\n- formulas as augmentation-attached semantic structures (`Provisional`).\n- spill-anchor/interior semantic relation concern (`Deferred`).\n- function-class taxonomy (pure/impure/volatile/external invalidation interaction) (`Provisional/Deferred`).\n- explicit region-node and error-reference first-class modeling (`Baseline` with `Provisional` details).\n- operations-only mutation discipline and replay/undo implications (`Baseline`).\n- Roslyn-inspired persistence facades and spine-respin semantics (`Baseline principle`, implementation shape `Exploratory`).\n\n### 7.2 Representation Strategy Design Space (`Exploratory`)\nRetained candidate families:\n- full-copy control baseline,\n- axis-maps + sparse cell-store,\n- persistent tile DAG,\n- row/column rope variants,\n- piece-table / patch-stack,\n- persistent rectangle algebra,\n- columnar chunk store,\n- balanced-block measured hybrid.\n\nRetained evaluation/benchmark framing:\n- common prototype contract,\n- workload corpus (synthetic + trace-driven),\n- instrumentation metrics (latency, memory, reuse ratio, read amplification),\n- correctness/invariant checks,\n- weighted downselect process.\n\n### 7.3 Deferred/Open Decision Register (`Deferred`)\nPriority decision buckets:\n- ID/address policy (`RowId`/`ColId` scope, reuse, canonical address text).\n- value algebra/coercion/lifting policy.\n- dynamic-reference (`INDIRECT`-class) dependency policy.\n- OpLog granularity and compaction boundaries.\n- iterative-cycle defaults.\n- post-server-sequenced collaboration conflict semantics.\n\n### 7.4 Suggested Kickoff Work Items (`Provisional`)\nOCaml:\n- implement core ADTs and `apply_op` skeleton with phase deltas.\n\nLean:\n- encode core ADTs and start theorem backlog:\n  - replay determinism,\n  - structural rewrite totality/invalidation coverage,\n  - no hidden green-state mutation.\n\nPacks/evidence implications retained:\n- structural rewrite classification trace requirements,\n- cycle iteration trace requirements,\n- binder-normalized-reference and error-reference persistence checks.\n\n## 8. Core vs Non-core Interaction Summary\nNon-core layers and their core interaction contracts:\n- UI/TUI: consume snapshots/deltas, dispatch operations; no hidden core mutation path.\n- File adapters: translate external formats to/from core operations and entities; preserve degradation signaling.\n- VBA/macro hosts: execute outside core, feed core through explicit operation pathway.\n- Collaboration adapters: replicate operation envelopes with causality/idempotency metadata.\n\n## 9. Uncertainty and Quality Guardrails\n- Uncertain and vague material is intentionally retained with explicit tags; this avoids knowledge loss while preventing accidental elevation to baseline semantics.\n- Any baseline mutation must specify:\n  - affected invariants,\n  - affected trace artifacts,\n  - pack/gate implications,\n  - migration impact on Rust/.NET/OCaml/Lean projections.\n\n## 10. Source Coverage Index (No-Loss Map)\nThis index asserts where each source family is captured in this document.\n\n- `ARCHITECTURE_AND_REQUIREMENTS.md` core sections (`3.1`, `3.2`, `3.3`, `3.4`, `3.6.1`, `3.11`..`3.17`): Sections `5` and `6`.\n- `notes/BRAINSTORM_NOTES.md` core-related motifs/open questions: Sections `7.1`, `7.3`, `8`.\n- `notes/archive/formal-model/FORMAL_MODELS_IDEAS.md` layered model + design-space ideas + benchmarking: Sections `6`, `7.1`, `7.2`.\n- `notes/archive/formal-model/FORMAL_MODEL_REMAINING_NOTES.md` deferred decisions: Section `7.3`.\n- `notes/archive/formal-model/FORMAL_CORE_STATUS_AND_SUGGESTIONS_DRAFT.md` status-derived priorities + kickoff suggestions: Sections `7.4`, `7.3`.\n\n## 11. Superseded Notes and Active Working Rule\nActive formal-model work must happen in this document.\n\nSuperseded note paths (`notes/FORMAL_*.md`) remain as archive redirects.\nFull historical copies are retained under `notes/archive/formal-model/`.\r\n\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}} {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_9","type":"command_execution","command":"\"C:\\\\Program Files\\\\PowerShell\\\\7\\\\pwsh.exe\" -Command 'Get-Content -Raw \"prompts/runs/20260308-171858-core-engine-fec-f3e-deep-research-pack-01/inputs/source/foundation-core__CHARTER.md\"'","aggregated_output":"# CHARTER.md — DNA Calc Charter\r\n\r\n## 1. Mission\r\n**DNA Calc Mission**  \r\nDNA Calc is a near-formal, state-of-the-art spreadsheet platform that matches Excel’s behavior with high fidelity while remaining rigorously specified, verifiable, and built to evolve. It is a clean-room implementation based only on public documentation, published research, and reproducible observation of Excel’s behavior—never proprietary sources or reverse-engineering of internals. DNA Calc prioritizes correctness under concurrency, graceful degradation across versions and profiles, and stable, versioned interfaces that support a long-lived ecosystem of tools, add-ins, and automation.\r\n\r\n## 2. Doctrine\r\nDoctrine is mandatory operating guidance. **Hygiene** is listed before **Evolution**.\r\n\r\n### 2.1 Hygiene Doctrine\r\n1. **Spec-first (or spec-with) changes**  \r\n   Behavior changes are not “done” until spec + cases are updated and gates pass.\r\n2. **Computed obligations, no hand-waving**  \r\n   Required checks are determined mechanically from diffs and profiles; bypass is rare and explicit.\r\n3. **One-command readiness**  \r\n   `meta check` (or equivalent) is the standard way to declare a state “green.”\r\n4. **Regressions are assets**  \n   Every bug becomes a minimized, machine-replayable trace/case artifact and remains in the corpus.\n5. **Determinism-first debugging**  \n   Deterministic modes exist for triage and conformance runs; non-determinism is opt-in and labeled.\n6. **Evidence discipline**  \n   Every Excel-compatibility claim is backed by public sources and/or a reproducible observation harness.\n7. **Coupled assurance stack**  \n   Claims of correctness must stay coupled across semantics, proofs/models, executable tests, and CI gate artifacts.\n\r\n### 2.2 Evolution Doctrine\r\n1. **Profiles are the semantics spine**  \r\n   Documents bind to `profile_id` + `profile_version`. Breaking semantic changes require profile bumps.\r\n2. **Protocols are versioned and negotiated**  \r\n   OpLog, engine protocol surface, automation protocol, UI protocol—each has explicit versions and negotiation.\r\n3. **Degrade gracefully; never crash**  \r\n   Unsupported or unknown features must preserve data (opaque) and/or surface explicit deterministic errors/warnings.\r\n4. **Unknown parts round-trip**  \r\n   When feasible, unrecognized file parts and extension payloads are preserved byte-for-byte.\r\n5. **Migrations are explicit and replayable**  \n   Migrations are expressed as operations and are testable/replayable.\n6. **No hidden mutation pathways**  \n   All persistent state changes flow through the operation model; no “special cases” for UI, scripts, VBA, XLL, or collaboration.\n\n### 2.3 Named Program Principles\n1. **Alien Artifact leverage**\n   Use mathematically strong methods when they remove ambiguity and increase confidence, but keep each method tied to executable invariants and artifacts.\n2. **Design for Evolution**\n   Keep seams explicit, versioned, and negotiable so behavior can expand without semantic drift or silent breakage.\n\r\n## 3. Program Structure and Names\r\n### 3.1 System Family\r\n- **DNA Calc** (code token: `DnaCalc`) — the Goldilocks long-term foundation.\n- **DNA VisiCalc** (`DnaVisiCalc`) — Round 0 pathfinder.  \n  Functional scope authority for Round 0 is maintained in the DnaVisiCalc docs set (`SPEC_v0.md`, `ENGINE_REQUIREMENTS.md`, `ENGINE_API.md`); Foundation doctrine/process docs must remain consistent with that scope.\n- **DNA PreCalc** (`DnaPreCalc`) — Round 1 full end-to-end.\n- **DNA SuperCalc** (`DnaSuperCalc`) — Round 2 refactor/perfection pass.\n- **DNA Calc** (`DnaCalc`) — Round 3 synthesized long-term product.\n\r\n### 3.2 Team Colors\r\n- **Green** — Spec stack + verification: DSLs, Lean proofs, TLA+ models, OCaml oracle, conformance packs.\r\n- **Red** — Rust implementation.\r\n- **Blue** — .NET implementation.\r\n\r\n(“Logistics” exists as a function; it may be implemented primarily in .NET tooling.)\r\n\r\n## 4. Clean-room Rule (Non-negotiable)\r\nDNA Calc development relies only on:\r\n- public specifications and documentation,\r\n- published research,\r\n- reproducible observation of Excel behavior.\r\n\r\nExcluded:\r\n- proprietary code, restricted materials, decompilation/disassembly of Excel internals, or reverse engineering of internals.\r\n\r\n## 5. Glossary (short)\n- **Profile**: A versioned semantics bundle defining meaning, compatibility rules, and required obligation packs.\n- **OpLog**: The operation log—the single representation of persistent state changes.\n- **Epoch / Meta-epoch**: Epoch versions document state; meta-epochs version stabilized project states (profiles + packs + implementations).\n- **Calc stabilized**: A runtime/data state where derived outputs are complete for a declared scope and epoch.\n- **Profile green**: A release/readiness state where all required obligation packs for a profile are passing.\n- **Stabilized epoch**: The latest epoch whose derived values are complete for a declared scope.\n- **Meta-epoch commit**: A published stabilization checkpoint containing capability manifest, conformance report, and regression updates.\n- **Artifact freeze**: A required set of versioned artifacts that must be locked before advancing rounds or declaring a profile green.\n- **External update op**: An explicit OpLog operation representing inbound STREAM/RTD-like value changes.\n- **Stream semantics version**: A profile-scoped version token controlling STREAM/external update behavior.\n- **Stream replay bundle**: A versioned artifact containing topic declarations and ordered update envelopes for deterministic replay.\n- **Degradation class**: Policy outcome for unsupported behavior (`Native`, `Lowered`, `Opaque`, `Rejected`).\n- **Obligation pack**: A computed set of checks required to claim readiness for a profile.\n- **Capability manifest**: Runtime/Build metadata describing supported protocols, profiles, features, and pack results.\n\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}} {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_12","type":"agent_message","text":"The pack does include mirrored `foundation-core__README.md` and `foundation-core__OPERATIONS.md`, so I’m using those as the repo-doctrine equivalent within the user’s source-only boundary. {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_15","type":"command_execution","command":"\"C:\\\\Program Files\\\\PowerShell\\\\7\\\\pwsh.exe\" -Command 'Get-Content -Raw \"prompts/runs/20260308-171858-core-engine-fec-f3e-deep-research-pack-01/inputs/source/foundation-core__OPERATIONS.md\"'","aggregated_output":"# OPERATIONS.md — DNA Calc Operations\r\n\r\n## 1. Purpose\r\nOperations defines how DNA Calc is developed, stabilized, and evolved under the Mission and Doctrine. It is designed to withstand “agentic coding weather” and frequent plan changes.\r\n\r\n## 2. Structure (Green/Red/Blue) and Veto\r\n### 2.1 Responsibilities\r\n**Green (Spec & Assurance)**\n- Owns DSL specs, proofs, protocol specs, OCaml oracle, cases, conformance packs.\n- Owns profile definitions, profile versioning rules, and negotiation schemas.\n- Owns `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` as the detailed formal-semantics working document.\n- Curates minimized regressions and “known quirks” documentation.\n- **Has veto on declaring a profile “stabilized/green.”**\n\r\n**Red (Rust engine)**\r\n- Implements the protocol surface and passes Green’s packs.\r\n- Focuses on hard-rock correctness under concurrency and performance scaling.\r\n\r\n**Blue (.NET engine)**\r\n- Implements the identical protocol surface and passes Green’s packs.\r\n- Provides independent confirmation of spec clarity and implementation feasibility.\r\n\r\n**Logistics (Tooling & Coordination)**\r\n- Builds and maintains `meta` orchestration, CI wiring, artifact storage/reporting, dashboards.\r\n- Keeps cycle time low and reproducibility high.\r\n- Generally .NET-first tooling, with small Rust utilities when warranted.\r\n\r\n### 2.2 Module Triads\r\nEvery major spec/module area is managed by a **triad**:\r\n- **Design** representative (spec intent and structure),\r\n- **Assurance** representative (packs, proofs, test strategy),\r\n- **Delivery** representative (Red/Blue implementation implications).\r\n\r\nTriads resolve ambiguity early and ensure artifacts remain consistent.\r\n\r\n## 3. Development Recalc Cycle (mirrors calculation)\nThe project evolves through a “recalc” cycle operating on DAGs.\n\r\n### 3.1 DAGs\r\n- **Spec DAG**: Lean modules, TLA+ modules, schemas/IDL, profiles.\r\n- **Assurance DAG**: obligation packs, oracle runs, minimized cases, perf signatures.\r\n- **Implementation DAG**: Red modules, Blue modules, adapters, protocol surfaces.\r\n- **Interop DAG** (later/heavier): Excel differential packs, file adapter conformance.\r\n\r\n### 3.2 Phase Model\r\n1. **Edit / Dirty Marking**\n   - Any change marks a set of nodes dirty (modules, packs, profiles, protocols), including `OpExternalUpdate`.\n2. **Dependency Closure**\n   - `meta` computes impacted obligations and required version bumps.\n3. **Scheduling**\r\n   - Work is dispatched along parallel tracks: Design / Assurance / Delivery / Logistics.\r\n4. **Execution**\n   - Proofs/model checks, oracle runs, engine conformance, perf signatures.\n   - For behavior-sensitive changes, execute a coupled evidence lane: semantics/spec delta, proof/model output, deterministic replay artifacts, and scaling signature evidence.\n5. **Stabilization**\r\n   - A profile is stabilized when its required obligation packs pass and artifacts are emitted.\r\n6. **Meta-epoch Commit**\n   - Publish capability manifest + conformance report + regression updates.\n\n### 3.3 Sequence-Only Planning Doctrine (No Time Schedules)\nProject execution planning is sequence-based, not calendar-based.\n\nMandatory rule:\n1. Plans and execution notes must not use date-based or duration-based commitments (for example deadlines, ETA dates, week-based schedules, or \"finish by X time\").\n2. Execution order must be expressed using:\n   - priority,\n   - dependency/`depends_on` relations,\n   - blocker status,\n   - gate/pack readiness criteria,\n   - risk and impact ordering.\n3. Progress is reported by state transitions and obligation closure (for example `planned -> in_progress -> blocked -> complete`), not by elapsed time targets.\n4. If temporal metadata is captured for audit/provenance (timestamps in artifacts), it is observational only and must not be used as a planning commitment axis.\n\r\n### 3.4 Auto vs Manual Modes (dev analogy)\n- **Auto**: CI runs the full required packs for affected profiles.\r\n- **Manual**: local development may defer heavy packs, but merge requires stabilization.\r\n\r\n## 4. Obligation Packs and Gates\r\n### 4.1 Packs\nPacks are the unit of readiness (examples):\n- `PACK.visicalc.core` (Pathfinder semantics)\n- `PACK.concurrent.epochs` (TLA+ invariants and schedule tests)\n- `PACK.udf.basic` (external UDF registration + calls)\n- `PACK.lean.ocaml.alignment.core` (Lean-bounded fixtures aligned with OCaml oracle outcomes)\n- `PACK.stream.basic` (STREAM/external updates propagation)\n- `PACK.stream.oracle.diff` (cross-engine + oracle stream replay parity)\n- `PACK.structural.insert` (insert row/col rewrite + traces)\n- `PACK.ui.viewport` (geometry/hit-test invariants + RenderPlan checks)\n- `PACK.scaling.signature` (growth suite and slope reporting)\n- `PACK.interop.degrade_matrix` (Native/Lowered/Opaque/Rejected policy conformance)\n- `PACK.interop.roundtrip.opaque` (unknown-part round-trip guarantees)\n- `PACK.collab.replication.core` (OpLog replication envelope and idempotency checks)\n\nAdditional Round 1 candidate packs informed by pathfinder evidence:\n- `PACK.control.basic` (control definition, validation, dependency participation)\n- `PACK.chart.basic` (chart definition, sink-node evaluation, chart-output determinism)\n- `PACK.calcdelta.basic` (typed delta entries, epoch tagging, emission/drain semantics)\n- `PACK.volatility.three_cat` (Standard/Volatile/ExternallyInvalidated invalidation behavior)\n\nPack status terminology:\n- `exercised`: implementation-level behavior exists with local tests.\n- `green-validated`: Green-owned pack artifacts and required conformance evidence are complete.\n- `exercised` is not sufficient for stabilization claims.\n\n### 4.2 Gate Rules\n- A profile cannot be declared “stabilized” unless all required packs for that profile are green.\n- Failing packs must generate or update minimized cases.\n- Required packs for a claim must include triangulation evidence across OCaml oracle, Red (Rust), and Blue (.NET) where applicable.\n- STREAM readiness for Round 0 requires `PACK.stream.basic` and stream cases in `PACK.concurrent.epochs`.\n\n### 4.3 Pack Contract Discipline\n- Every pack publishes:\n  - scope, required fixtures, deterministic mode requirements, pass/fail thresholds, emitted artifacts.\n- `PACK.scaling.signature` contract must include:\n  - required workload families, phase-level counter schema, slope calculation method, regression thresholds.\n- `PACK.concurrent.epochs` contract must include:\n  - tiered model-check configurations and archived minimized counterexample traces.\n- `PACK.visicalc.core` contract must include:\n  - minimum semantic coverage and required artifact set for Round 0 profile readiness.\n\r\n## 5. Regression Handling (AAR-driven)\r\n- Every failure produces:\r\n  - a minimized trace,\r\n  - a conformance report entry,\r\n  - a triage record (root cause classification).\r\n- Periodic AARs consolidate learnings into:\r\n  - refined packs,\r\n  - clarified profile specs,\r\n  - updated doctrine (rare).\r\n\r\n## 6. Tooling Interface Rules\n- Cross-language integration is file/CLI-based (schemas, traces, manifests).\n- OCaml oracle runs as CLI.\n- Lean and TLC run under orchestration; no manual “tribal incantations.”\n\n### 6.1 Meta CLI Contract\nCanonical command families include:\n- `meta check`\n- `meta resolve`\n- `meta run-pack`\n- `meta report`\n- `meta pin-profile`\n\nEach command must emit machine-readable artifacts suitable for CI gating and local replay.\n\n### 6.2 Obligation Resolver Semantics\n- Resolver inputs include:\n  - changed files, profile definitions, pack declarations, capability manifests, previous pack fingerprints.\n- Resolver outputs include:\n  - impacted obligation closure, execution plan, cache hit/miss report, required version-bump notes.\n- Caching must be fingerprinted and deterministic; cache reuse is allowed only on matching fingerprints and schema versions.\n\n### 6.3 Local vs CI Modes\n- Local mode may skip heavyweight packs for cycle-time, but must still compute full impacted closure.\n- CI mode executes full required closure and is the authority for merge readiness.\n\n### 6.4 Tooling Language Policy\n- Repository tooling implementations are .NET-first (C# or F#) unless explicitly approved otherwise.\n- Python is not an allowed tooling implementation language in this repository by default.\n- Any Python exception requires an explicit logged approval record, including:\n  - scope and owner,\n  - rationale for exception,\n  - sunset/replacement plan.\n- PowerShell (`pwsh`) is permitted for convenience orchestration and launcher scripts, but behavior-critical tool runtime logic (for example empirical Excel driving and artifact emission) must remain in stable .NET tools.\n\r\n## 7. Deliverable Names per Round\n- Round 0: **DnaVisiCalc** (Pathfinder) — proves the verification + meta-control loop.\n- Round 1: **DnaPreCalc** — first full end-to-end implementation and spec push.\n- Round 2: **DnaSuperCalc** — refactor/polish and “too-perfect” exploration.\n- Round 3: **DnaCalc** — streamlined, maintainable Goldilocks product.\n\n### 7.1 Round 0 Functional Scope Authority\n- For DnaVisiCalc pathfinder v0 functional-scope questions, use:\n  - `..\\\\DnaVisiCalc\\\\docs\\\\SPEC_v0.md`\n  - `..\\\\DnaVisiCalc\\\\docs\\\\ENGINE_REQUIREMENTS.md`\n  - `..\\\\DnaVisiCalc\\\\docs\\\\ENGINE_API.md`\n- Foundation docs remain the source of truth for doctrine, architecture framing, and operations process, and must stay consistent with that upstream functional contract.\n- Proposed functional-scope expansions discovered in implementation (for example from gap-analysis style docs) are tracked as follow-on backlog and routed through synthesis before any doctrine/policy promotion.\n\n## 8. Managed-Run Discipline (Prompt, Research, Synthesis, Reference)\nPrompt execution, deep research, synthesis, and reference-spec processing are treated as managed operational activities with run artifacts.\n\n### 8.0 Managed-Run Task Types\n- `prompt_run`: reusable prompt execution runs under `prompts/runs/<run-id>/`.\n- `research_run`: deep research runs under `research/runs/<run-id>/`.\n- `synthesis_run`: synthesis/decision runs under `synthesis/runs/<run-id>/`.\n- `reference_run`: reference-spec processing and conformance-candidate extraction runs under `reference/runs/<run-id>/`.\n\n### 8.1 Prompt Runs\n- Prompt-run operating procedure lives in `prompts/README.md`.\n- Prompt runs must capture raw outputs, manifests, and trace artifacts under `prompts/runs/<run-id>/`.\n- Prompt outputs are inputs to decision-making, not source-of-truth policy.\n\n### 8.2 Synthesis Runs\n- Synthesis-run operating procedure lives in `synthesis/README.md`.\n- Synthesis runs must record per-suggestion decisions (`accept` / `adapt` / `defer` / `reject`) with rationale and target-document references.\n- No synthesis edit should be applied without a corresponding decision-log record.\n- Synthesis artifacts are audit/history records; source-of-truth remains `CHARTER.md`, `ARCHITECTURE_AND_REQUIREMENTS.md`, `OPERATIONS.md`, and `notes/RESEARCH_NOTES.md` for non-doctrinal retained knowledge.\n\n### 8.3 Research Runs\n- Deep-research prompt templates live in `prompts/PROMPT_PACK_DEEP_RESEARCH.md`.\n- Research topic and source registries live under `research/`.\n- Research runs must capture exact prompt input text, source links, and output artifacts under `research/runs/<run-id>/`.\n- Research outputs are evidence inputs; they do not become doctrine until synthesized into source-of-truth docs.\n\n### 8.4 Document Precedence During Synthesis\nWhen synthesis suggestions conflict, precedence remains:\n1. `CHARTER.md`\n2. `ARCHITECTURE_AND_REQUIREMENTS.md`\n3. `OPERATIONS.md`\n4. `notes/RESEARCH_NOTES.md`\n5. `notes/BRAINSTORM_NOTES.md`\n\n### 8.5 Synthesis Completion and Status Model\n- A synthesis run is complete only when all of the following exist:\n  - frozen input hashes,\n  - full suggestion index and decision log coverage for the scoped run set,\n  - applied/adapted changes reflected in source-of-truth docs,\n  - output synthesis report,\n  - source run manifests or registries marked `synthesized` with reference to the synthesis run id.\n- Suggested lifecycle states:\n  - `captured` (raw run outputs present),\n  - `synthesized` (decisions recorded and knowledge promoted),\n  - `archived` (run retained for audit/history, no longer active working set).\n\n### 8.6 Working Directory Semantics\n- `prompts/runs/*`, `research/runs/*`, `synthesis/runs/*`, and `reference/runs/*` are managed run evidence directories.\n- Their outputs must be assumed non-authoritative until synthesis promotion.\n- After synthesis, these directories remain audit inputs; day-to-day guidance comes from source-of-truth docs and `notes/RESEARCH_NOTES.md`.\n- Temporary agent-generated files should default to a repository-local `.tmp/` directory that is `.gitignore`d.\n- Prefer repository-local `.tmp/` over OS user temp directories unless an explicit task requires system temp location semantics.\n\n### 8.7 Pathfinder Feedback Pattern\n- Pathfinder teams should follow a repeatable upstream-feedback loop:\n  1. implement against Foundation docs,\n  2. capture gaps/ambiguities from implementation reality,\n  3. document local evidence and proposal set,\n  4. route proposals through synthesis (`accept` / `adapt` / `defer` / `reject`) before doctrine promotion.\n- Pathfinder feedback documents are proposal inputs, not source-of-truth edits by themselves.\n- Proposal sets should include target-section references, rationale, and dependency notes to support deterministic synthesis decisions.\n\n### 8.8 Legacy Guidance Hand-off\n- When an upstream pathfinder guide is superseded (for example gap-analysis/proposal/mapping docs), synthesis must do one of:\n  - promote the relevant content into source-of-truth docs, or\n  - retain it explicitly in Foundation notes as deferred backlog with rationale.\n- Current retained example: `notes/VISICALC_V0_SCOPE_ALIGNMENT_NOTES.md`.\n- Superseded guides can then be archived without losing material planning knowledge.\n\n### 8.9 Reference Spec Processing and Conformance Extraction\n- Raw external-spec mirrors remain under `reference/downloads/` with indexed provenance in `reference/index.csv`.\n- Managed spec-processing runs emit normalized reference artifacts under `reference/runs/<run-id>/outputs/` and are the preferred input layer for conformance extraction and formal-model cross-referencing work.\n- Required processed-run outputs:\n  - `run_manifest.json`,\n  - `documents.csv`,\n  - `selected_sources.csv`,\n  - `spec_items.jsonl`,\n  - `conformance_items.jsonl`,\n  - `conformance_excluded.jsonl`,\n  - `llm/classification_tasks.jsonl`,\n  - per-document `document_manifest.json`, `segments.jsonl`, `sentences.jsonl`, `spec_items.jsonl`, `conformance_candidates.jsonl`, and `conformance_excluded.jsonl`.\n- Every extracted segment/sentence/conformance item must retain source back-references (source URL, mirrored local path, and finest available anchor such as page/section/table/cell/image reference).\n- Coverage must be explicit: if any source artifact cannot be fully extracted (for example OCR-pending PDF), the run must emit pending markers/counters rather than silently dropping content.\n- LLM-assisted classification is allowed only as an auditable layer on top of deterministic extraction; prompts/responses or imported classifier outputs must be captured as run artifacts.\n- Detailed format contract for this layer is maintained in `REFERENCE_SPEC_FORMAT_AND_CONFORMANCE.md`.\n\n### 8.10 Empirical Findings as Reference-Conformance Inputs\n- Empirical run outputs under `research/runs/<run-id>/` remain working evidence by default; they do not become standing conformance references automatically.\n- High-value empirical observations (for example behavior that resolves spec ambiguity, contradicts expectation, or materially constrains compatibility design) must be curated into `reference/empirical/` as promoted finding records (`findings_registry.jsonl` + human index/docs).\n- Promoted empirical findings are first-class conformance-source inputs alongside processed spec items, with explicit provenance links back to:\n  - research run id and scenario/task id,\n  - captured evidence artifacts,\n  - Excel build/version metadata and `EXCEL.EXE` hash,\n  - runner/tool version and source revision.\n- Empirical promotions must be selective: not every executed probe is promoted; only findings with durable conformance relevance.\n- Conformance requirement synthesis must allow source binding to either:\n  - spec-derived evidence (`SPEC-*` lineage), or\n  - empirical-derived evidence (`EMP-*` lineage),\n  and should support mixed-source justification where both apply.\n\n### 8.11 Cross-Repo Lane Handoff Template (Normative)\nWhen sibling lane repos propose cross-program policy text for Foundation adoption, include a minimal handoff record in the managed run:\n1. scope and profile bounds:\n   - affected domains, requirement families, and profile/version applicability.\n2. proposed normative text:\n   - exact candidate text and target artifact paths/sections.\n3. evidence and replay links:\n   - source/evidence ids, run ids, and replay artifact locations.\n4. unresolved decisions and risk impact:\n   - explicit open decisions, blocker status, and failure/risk impact if deferred.\n\nHandoff records may be included as:\n1. a dedicated `outputs/HANDOFF_<lane>.md`, or\n2. a structured section inside synthesis decision logs.\n\n## 9. Clean-room Evidence Workflow\n- Compatibility claims require an evidence record that includes:\n  - claim identifier, linked REQ/INT/REAL IDs, admissible source type, capture/reproduction steps, reviewer decision.\n- Admissible evidence sources:\n  - public documentation/specifications,\n  - published research,\n  - reproducible black-box observation harness outputs.\n- Non-admissible sources:\n  - proprietary or restricted materials,\n  - reverse-engineered internals.\n- Evidence review is a gate input for stabilization claims involving compatibility behavior.\n\n## 10. Round Progression and Exit Coupling\n- Round progression is coupled to artifact freezes and required pack sets.\n- Minimum exit artifacts per round include:\n  - capability manifest,\n  - conformance report,\n  - updated minimized regression corpus,\n  - pack result index for required profiles.\n- Round transitions are blocked when required artifacts or pack obligations are missing.\n\n### 10.1 Round Exit Track Decomposition\n- For planning clarity, round-progress reports may decompose work into:\n  - Track A: implementation scope,\n  - Track B: formal/assurance obligations,\n  - Track C: beyond-minimum exploratory artifacts.\n- Decomposition is informational; gate authority remains required artifacts and required packs.\n\n### 10.2 Open Decisions Register\n- Open cross-team policy decisions are tracked as `DEC-###` entries with:\n  - owner, target round, current status, blocking impact.\n- No critical ambiguity should remain implicit in brainstorm-only notes once it affects stabilization criteria.\n\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}} {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_16","type":"command_execution","command":"\"C:\\\\Program Files\\\\PowerShell\\\\7\\\\pwsh.exe\" -Command 'Get-Content -Raw \"prompts/runs/20260308-171858-core-engine-fec-f3e-deep-research-pack-01/inputs/source/fec-f3e-current-spec__ENGINE_FEC_F3E_REDESIGN_SPEC.md\"'","aggregated_output":"# FEC/F3E Redesign Specification (Plan B, b4)\n\n## 1. Purpose\nActive internal FEC/F3E seam contract for `dnavisicalc-core-fml`.\n\nTransaction lane:\n1. `prepare`\n2. `open_session` + `capability_view`\n3. `execute`\n4. `commit`\n\n## 2. Version and Scope\n- Interface marker: `fec-f3e-redesign/b4` (`src/fec_f3e/spec.rs`).\n- Trace schema marker: `fec-f3e-trace/b4` (`src/fec_f3e/trace.rs`).\n- In scope: internal seam semantics, engine integration policy, test/evidence lane.\n\n## 3. Identity Contract (b4)\nStable IDs are first-class seam identity:\n- `FecFormulaId` with `stable_id()`.\n- `FecNameId` for name dependency identity.\n- `FecRangeId` for spill-range identity.\n\nNames are metadata in traces (`name_label`) but no longer contractual dependency identity strings.\n\n## 4. Commit Delta Contract (b4)\n`CommitResult` now separates mutation classes:\n- `value_delta: FecValueDelta`\n- `shape_delta: FecShapeDelta`\n- `topology_delta: FecTopologyDelta`\n\n`topology_delta` carries:\n- dependency set deltas (`cells`, `name_ids`, `spill_children`),\n- impacted cell/name-id sets,\n- typed impact class (`None|DependencySetChanged|SpillRangeChanged|SpillBlocked`).\n\n## 5. Spill Event Contract (b4)\nSpill transitions are explicit event objects in `shape_delta.spill_event`:\n- `SpillTakeover`\n- `SpillClearance`\n- `SpillBlocked`\n\nEach includes affected range/anchor context and invalidation scope.\nEngine spill scheduler hints are derived from these events (FEC emits metadata; policy remains engine-owned).\n\n## 6. Snapshot and Capability Semantics\n- Sessions bind `(formula_id, expected_token?, snapshot_epoch)`.\n- `commit` enforces:\n  - tx/session snapshot equality,\n  - coordinator snapshot fence equality.\n- Capability decisions are session-bound during `capability_view` and commit-validated.\n- Reject outcomes remain machine-typed (`CommitStatus` + `CommitRejectDetail`).\n\n## 7. Incremental Policy Boundary\nFEC provides topology evidence; engine owns recalc policy.\n\nb4 engine integration now consumes runtime name-id deltas in incremental routing:\n- dirty-name updates are eligible for incremental recalc,\n- no automatic “name edit => full recalc” at seam policy boundary.\n\nThis keeps full-recalc policy above FEC/F3E, consistent with core-engine ownership.\n\n## 8. Trace Schema Contract (b4)\nWhen `DNAVISICALC_FEC_F3E_TRACE=1`:\n- each event includes `trace_version=fec-f3e-trace/b4`,\n- trace emitter validates field keys (non-empty, unique) and emits `schema_valid`.\n\nKey b4 commit fields:\n- `formula_stable_id`\n- `shape_delta`\n- `topology_impact`\n- `value_changed`\n- dependency delta counts\n- snapshot/capability reject context\n\n## 9. Perf Scaffolding (b4)\n`FecSeamPerfCounters` now includes explicit spill-event counters:\n- `spill_takeover_count`\n- `spill_clearance_count`\n- `spill_blocked_count`\n\nEngine API remains:\n- `fec_seam_perf_snapshot()`\n- `reset_fec_seam_perf_counters()`\n\n## 10. Validation Lane\nPrimary seam scenario suite:\n- `crates/dnavisicalc-core-fml/tests/fec_f3e_seam_scenarios_tests.rs`\n\nb4 targeted scenarios include:\n- name-id incremental routing on dirty-name edits,\n- spill blocked -> recovery flow,\n- end-to-end spill fail/recovery/re-fail with dependent observer updates,\n- external scheduler handoff and conservative fallback behavior.\n\nFoundation handoff prompt:\n- `docs/ENGINE_FEC_F3E_FOUNDATION_UPDATED_SPEC_POINTERS_PROMPT.md`\n\n## 11. Open Items\n1. Threaded coordinator implementation (`Arc`/sync primitives) for concurrent scheduler pilots.\n2. Deterministic contention replay pack for commit conflict interleavings.\n3. Higher-fidelity causal callgraph derivation (current graph is adjacency-derived).\n\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}} {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_17","type":"agent_message","text":"I have the baseline doctrine, core formal model, and the current FEC/F3E contract loaded. and Runtime Evidence (Compact)\n\n## Purpose\nThis compact source replaces large Rust implementation and raw trace files for design-phase synthesis. It retains the seam contract shape, runtime behaviors, and empirically observed boundary-event patterns.\n\n## Replaced Sources\n- `fec-f3e-implementation__contracts.rs`\n- `fec-f3e-implementation__f3e_engine.rs`\n- `fec-f3e-implementation__fec_host.rs`\n- `fec-f3e-implementation__engine.rs`\n- `fec-f3e-implementation__eval.rs`\n- `fec-f3e-implementation__trace.rs`\n- `fec-f3e-implementation__fec_f3e_seam_scenarios_tests.rs`\n- `fec-f3e-evidence__seam_trace.event_counts.tsv`\n- `fec-f3e-evidence__seam_trace.callgraph.edges.csv`\n\n## Transactional Seam Shape (Implemented)\n1. `prepare(formula_text,bounds,ctx) -> FormulaPlan`\n2. `install_plan(formula_id,plan) -> FormulaToken`\n3. `open_session(formula_id,expected_token,snapshot_epoch) -> EvalSessionId`\n4. `capability_view(session_id,formula_id,required_caps) -> FecCapabilityView`\n5. `execute(evaluator,EvalRequest) -> EvalTransaction`\n6. `commit(EvalTransaction) -> CommitResult`\n\nCoordinator epoch is explicitly tracked and checked at commit time against session snapshot.\n\n## Key Contract Types (Current)\n- Stable identity: `FecNameId`, `FecRangeId`, `FecFormulaStableId`, `FecFormulaId`.\n- Capability model: `FecCapabilityTag`, `FecCapabilityDecision`, `FecCapabilityView`.\n- Eval envelope: `EvalRequest`, `EvalTransaction`, `F3eResultKind`, `EvalObservation`.\n- Dependency deltas: `F3eObservedDependencies`, `F3eDependencyDelta` (`cells`, `names`, `spill_children`).\n- Shape/topology deltas:\n  - `SpillDeltaEvent`: `None | SpillTakeover | SpillClearance | SpillBlocked`.\n  - `FecShapeDelta`, `FecTopologyDelta` with `TopologyImpact`.\n- Commit outcome:\n  - `CommitStatus` with applied/rejected taxonomy,\n  - `CommitRejectDetail` with structured reject metadata,\n  - split payload: `value_delta`, `shape_delta`, `topology_delta`.\n- Perf counters: seam call counts, reject counters, dependency/spill delta totals.\n\n## Implemented Failure Semantics\n`commit` reject classes are explicit and deterministic:\n- `RejectedSessionNotFound`\n- `RejectedFormulaNotRegistered`\n- `RejectedFormulaMismatch`\n- `RejectedExpectedTokenMismatch`\n- `RejectedTransactionTokenMismatch`\n- `RejectedCapabilityNotBound`\n- `RejectedCapabilityDecisionMismatch`\n- `RejectedCapabilityDenied`\n- `RejectedSnapshotConflict` (session mismatch and coordinator mismatch variants via reject detail code)\n\nReject payload fields include expected/actual token, expected/actual snapshot epoch, coordinator epoch, and denied capability.\n\n## Dependency + Spill Delta Sufficiency (Observed)\nCurrent deltas support policy-ready invalidation inputs:\n- runtime dependency changes on cells/names/spill-children,\n- explicit spill lifecycle events with entered/exited cells,\n- topology impact tags (`DependencySetChanged`, `SpillRangeChanged`, `SpillBlocked`).\n\nThis is sufficient for a scheduler to choose between selective invalidation and conservative fallback policies.\n\n## Engine Integration Signals\nThe engine integrates seam calls in both full and incremental recalc flows:\n- `evaluate_cell_via_f3e`\n- `evaluate_name_via_f3e`\n- `recalculate_full`\n- `recalculate_incremental`\n- `incremental_spill_fallback`\n\nEngine tracks `committed_epoch` and `stabilized_epoch`, applies runtime dependency deltas into reverse maps, and supports spill policy modes:\n- `ConservativeFullRecalc`\n- `ExternalScheduler`\n\n## Runtime Trace Schema\nTrace utilities emit boundary events with schema tag:\n- `trace_version=fec-f3e-trace/b4`\n- monotonic `seq`\n- `schema_valid` field-level validation indicator\n\n## Empirical Evidence Snapshot\nFrom seam event counts:\n- `fec.open_session`: 83\n- `fec.capability_view`: 83\n- `f3e.execute`: 83\n- `fec.commit`: 83\n- `f3e.prepare`: 36\n- `fec.install_plan`: 37\n- `engine.recalculate_full`: 50\n- `engine.recalculate_incremental`: 30\n- `engine.incremental_spill_fallback`: 1\n\nObserved stable phase pattern:\n- `fec.open_session -> fec.capability_view -> f3e.execute -> fec.commit -> engine.evaluate_cell_via_f3e`\n\n## Scenario Coverage (Seam Tests)\nImplemented scenario tests include:\n- static/no-dependency and static-reference formulas,\n- name-chain and name-delta routing,\n- dynamic reference retargeting (`INDIRECT`/`OFFSET` behavior),\n- spill takeover/clearance and spill blocked/recovery,\n- incremental dirty closure skip behavior,\n- recalc mode behavior (manual vs automatic),\n- policy signal tests for external-scheduler spill hints and conservative fallback,\n- perf snapshot scaffolding checks.\n\n## Design-Phase Takeaways\n1. Transaction boundary and reject semantics are concrete enough to synthesize normative contracts.\n2. Runtime deltas (dependency + spill shape + topology impact) are concrete enough to specify scheduler invalidation policy tiers.\n3. Epoch fencing and token checks are present, but broader multi-session contention/retry policy should still be made explicit in architecture docs.\n4. Formatting-sensitive formula dependencies and visibility-priority scheduling remain primarily architecture-level requirements; they are not the dominant seam signal in current traces.\r\n\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}} {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_19","type":"command_execution","command":"\"C:\\\\Program Files\\\\PowerShell\\\\7\\\\pwsh.exe\" -Command 'Get-Content -Raw \"prompts/runs/20260308-171858-core-engine-fec-f3e-deep-research-pack-01/inputs/source/dag-research-synthesis__10_conformance_and_proof_obligations.md\"'","aggregated_output":"# Conformance and Proof Obligations (Promoted Set)\n\n## Scope\nThis artifact converts reconciled DAG-theory findings into concrete candidate obligations for Green formalization and conformance-pack binding.\n\n## Proof obligations\n\n| Obligation ID | Statement (candidate theorem/property) | Assumptions/profile bounds | Source anchors | Candidate pack binding |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| DAG-PO-001 | Acyclic from-scratch equivalence: incremental recompute equals full recompute on acyclic dependency graphs | Static dependencies resolved; It captures unresolved architecture asks, stable decisions, and synthesis framing for the next core-engine design pass.\n\n## Replaced Sources\n- `foundation-design-draft__design_brief.md`\n- `foundation-design-draft__01_gap_map.md`\n- `foundation-design-draft__03_stable_topic_entries.md`\n- `foundation-design-draft__06_fec_f3e_b4_pointer_intake.md`\n- `foundation-notes__BRAINSTORM_NOTES.md`\n- `foundation-notes__RESEARCH_NOTES.md`\n- `dag-research-synthesis__01_scope_and_question_map.md`\n- `dag-research-synthesis__02_theory_and_math_catalog.md`\n- `dag-research-synthesis__03_algorithm_family_map.md`\n- `dag-research-synthesis__09_external_report_reconciliation.md`\n- `fec-f3e-current-spec__ENGINE_FEC_F3E_REDESIGN_SYNTHESIS.md`\n\n## Core Design Direction to Preserve\n1. Layered model:\n   - immutable structural model (green-tree style),\n   - structural dependency layer,\n   - calc-time dependency/reference overlay,\n   - spill/virtual-region overlay,\n   - pure-calc fast path when overlay mutation is not needed.\n2. FEC/F3E transactional seam as evaluation boundary.\n3. Async/high-concurrency recalc with epoch/MVCC and deterministic publish semantics.\n4. Explicit handling of formatting-sensitive calculations and visibility-priority scheduling as optional policy lanes.\n\n## Stable Decisions Captured\n### DEC-CALC-007 Formatting-Sensitive Overlay\n- Default: `TEXT(value, format_text)` depends on explicit format string, not ambient cell style.\n- Formula visibility of conditional-format effective style is profile-gated and provisional.\n- Add explicit formatting dependency tokens to calc-time overlay for formatting-observable functions.\n\n### DEC-CALC-008 Visibility Representation + Optional Visible-First\n- Model must include explicit visibility state (`visible_regions`, `visible_nodes`, `visibility_version`, priority policy).\n- Visibility can alter scheduling priority but not final semantics.\n- Deterministic queue key remains required (`priority_class`, topo order, stable node id).\n- Starvation prevention remains mandatory under visible-first policy.\n\n### DEC-CALC-009 FEC/F3E Seam Adoption Gate\n- Direction is conditional-go for transactional seam.\n- Required hardening themes:\n  - coordinator snapshot fencing,\n  - runtime name/spill dependency delta use in incremental invalidation,\n  - robust rejection taxonomy + structured details,\n  - spill selective invalidation with conservative fallback retained.\n\n## High-Impact Gaps to Resolve in Core Synthesis\n1. Precise structural graph vs calc-time overlay lifecycle and publish boundary.\n2. Overlay retention/eviction and epoch-safe garbage collection.\n3. Spill invalidation algebra for prior/current spill regions.\n4. Pure-calc fast-path guard conditions.\n5. Deterministic parallel reduction/tie-break rules.\n6. Function-catalog/profile-version invalidation triggers.\n7. Published-state semantics during in-flight recalculation.\n8. Exact FEC pre-resolution vs F3E semantic ownership boundary.\n\n## DAG Research Context to Retain\nResearch framing and prior reconciliation converge on:\n- staged adoption (baseline deterministic topo/SCC first, dynamic-topo/SAC next, differential or timely only where stream semantics justify complexity),\n- obligation-driven synthesis (proof/conformance and empirical packs),\n- explicit separation of dependency discovery vs execution ordering artifacts,\n- trace-first evidence discipline for dynamic dependency behavior.\n\n## Program-Level Constraints Carried Forward\n1. Deterministic replay artifacts are mandatory for conformance and regression work.\n2. Clean-room interop posture remains in force.\n3. Epoch-tagged stale/pending visibility is user-facing contract behavior.\n4. Controls/charts/names must remain first-class in engine architecture, not bolt-ons.\n5. Pack-contract coupling should be explicit in architecture text and test planning.\n\n## Open Questions to Keep Active\n1. How to define canonical publication ordering when value, dependency overlay, spill overlay, and formatting overlay all change in one transaction.\n2. Which deterministic replay schema becomes canonical across concurrent evaluator traces.\n3. How to encode degradation classes for dynamic reference and visibility-priority edge behavior.\n4. Which proofs are required for scheduler policy variation equivalence (`None` vs visible-first).\n\n## How to Use This Compact Doc\n- Treat this file as context and issue backlog for synthesis \"prompts/runs/20260308-171858-core-engine-fec-f3e-deep-research-pack-01/inputs/source/dag-research-synthesis__05_deep_research_synthesis.md\"'","aggregated_output":"# Deep Research Synthesis: DAG Computation Theory for Spreadsheet Engines\n\n## Executive synthesis\nThe strongest foundation for a spreadsheet-style DAG engine is not a single theory but a layered stack:\n1. **Graph-theoretic skeleton** (topological order + SCC decomposition),\n2. **Fixed-point semantics** for stabilization and cycle policy,\n3. **Incremental maintenance discipline** (dirty/stale/necessary + dynamic dependency handling),\n4. **Selective advanced incrementalization** (SAC/delta/differential) where cost justifies complexity.\n\nThis layered model aligns well with DNA Calc's existing architecture commitments (epochs, deterministic replay, profile-governed behavior) and gives clear proof/pack obligations.\n\n## Core architecture implications\n\n### 1) Separate semantic truth from scheduling mechanics\n- Semantic truth: fixed-point or acyclic evaluation result definition.\n- Scheduling mechanics: concrete algorithm (full rebuild, dynamic-topo, trace repair).\n\nWhy:\n- Enables swapping runtime algorithms without semantic drift.\n- Supports profile-based guarantees and deterministic mode.\n\n### 2) Make invalidation state a formal object\nAdopt explicit state model for each node/cell class:\n- `clean`, `stale`, `necessary`, `recomputed` (names can vary).\n\nWhy:\n- This is where most \"hidden behavior\" bugs occur.\n- SAC and Incremental literature show this is key to tractable debugging and analyzability.\n\n### 3) Treat dynamic dependencies as first-class\nDynamic references (INDIRECT-like, external lookups) should not be bolted onto static DAG assumptions.\n\nUse explicit lanes:\n- static dependencies (compile-time discoverable),\n- dynamic dependencies (evaluation-time observed, tokenized/provenance tracked).\n\n### 4) Reserve heavy math for high-leverage lanes\nNot every part needs full differential/timely machinery.\n\nRecommended:\n- baseline: graph + invalidation + deterministic scheduling,\n- advanced: delta/differential model for external streaming and high-update hotspots.\n\n## Theoretical \"best fit\" shortlist\n\n### A. Mandatory baseline theories\n1. Topological ordering and SCC decomposition.\n2. Fixed-point reasoning for cycle/iteration semantics.\n3. Deterministic replay and dependency soundness invariants.\n\n### B. High-value extension theories\n1. Self-adjusting computation for dynamic incremental repair.\n2. Dynamic cycle/topo maintenance for edit-heavy workloads.\n3. Incremental lambda-calculus / change-theory for function-level optimization and formal proofs.\n\n### C. Strategic future theories\n1. Timely/Differential model for external update semantics.\n2. Semiring provenance for explainability and algebraic trace reasoning.\n\n## Risks if theory is ignored\n1. Hidden non-determinism in parallel/invalidation handling.\n2. Over-recompute and performance collapse under dynamic references.\n3. Unprovable behavior drift between implementations.\n4. Inability to explain conflicting outputs in conformance investigations.\n\n## Recommended next execution sequence\n1. Formalize minimal DAG state transition model and invariants (clean/stale/necessary/recomputed).\n2. Add explicit conformance requirements for dynamic dependency token lifecycle.\n3. Build empirical comparison pack: full rebuild vs dynamic-topo maintenance.\n4. Add targeted SAC-inspired prototype for one dynamic dependency lane.\n5.
