Purpose\nThis document defines the current Round-0 core engine compatibility scope.\n\nIt is the top-priority repo-local specification for engine behavior.\n\nRepository integration scope (file adapter/TUI/repo crate boundaries) is maintained in `docs/SPEC_v0_INTEGRATION_APPENDIX.md`.\n\n## 2. Core Engine Scope\nThe engine contract is implementation-independent and covers:\n- deterministic, in-memory spreadsheet behavior,\n- host-driven mutation and recalculation APIs,\n- formula/value semantics,\n- structural rewrite semantics,\n- volatility/invalidation behavior,\n- UDF integration,\n- controls/charts/change tracking entities,\n- formatting and deterministic bulk enumeration surfaces.\n\n## 3. Normative v0 Engine Requirements\n\n### 3.1 Engine Boundary\n- Pure in-memory API.\n- Externally-driven execution only (no internal timers/threads/I/O).\n- Deterministic behavior for identical inputs and operation sequences.\n\n### 3.2 Sheet and Address Model\n- Single worksheet.\n- VisiCalc-size bounds by default: `A1..BK254` (63 columns x 254 rows).\n- A1 parsing supports:\n  - relative refs (`A1`),\n  - mixed absolute refs (`$A1`, `A$1`),\n  - fully absolute refs (`$A$1`),\n  - ranges (`A1:B7`, `A1...B7`).\n\n### 3.3 Cell and Name Inputs\n- Cells and names accept numeric, text, formula, and clear operations.\n- Names are case-insensitive, normalized uppercase, and validated against identifier/function/cell-ref conflicts.\n\n### 3.4 Formula Surface\n- Arithmetic, comparison, concatenation, and logical evaluation.\n- Dynamic arrays and spill semantics including spill references (`A1#`).\n- Explicit v0 function surface (aligned with current Rust baseline):\n  - aggregates: `SUM`, `MIN`, `MAX`, `AVERAGE`, `COUNT`\n  - conditional/error handling: `IF`, `IFERROR`, `IFNA`, `NA`, `ERROR`\n  - logical predicates: `AND`, `OR`, `NOT`, `ISERROR`, `ISNA`, `ISBLANK`, `ISTEXT`, `ISNUMBER`, `ISLOGICAL`, `ERROR.TYPE`\n  - core math/scientific: `ABS`, `INT`, `ROUND`, `SIGN`, `SQRT`, `EXP`, `LN`, `LOG10`, `SIN`, `COS`, `TAN`, `ATN`, `PI`\n  - financial/lookup: `NPV`, `PV`, `FV`, `PMT`, `LOOKUP`\n  - text: `CONCAT`, `LEN`\n  - dynamic arrays and lambda family: `SEQUENCE`, `RANDARRAY`, `LET`, `LAMBDA`, `MAP`\n  - reference helpers: `INDIRECT`, `OFFSET`, `ROW`, `COLUMN`\n  - volatile/stream: `NOW`, `RAND`, `STREAM`\n- Stage priority for non-Rust implementations:\n  - critical first: `LET`, `LAMBDA`, `MAP`, `INDIRECT`, `OFFSET`, `SEQUENCE`, `RANDARRAY`, `STREAM`\n  - lower priority in staged runs: trig/scientific and financial/lookup categories, but still part of the target v0 surface.\n- `STREAM` semantics are explicit (not Excel-derived):\n  - call shape: `STREAM(period_secs [, LAMBDA(counter)])`,\n  - `period_secs` must be a positive number,\n  - stream state is per formula cell; return value is that cell's stream counter (starting at `0`),\n  - stream counters advance only through host-driven `tick_streams(elapsed_secs)` invalidation,\n  - no autonomous/background ticking inside the engine,\n  - if lambda is provided, it is applied to the counter and may return scalar or array.\n- `LAMBDA`/`MAP` are fully dynamic-array aware:\n  - lambda parameters may receive scalar or array values,\n  - `MAP` must support array broadcasting across inputs,\n  - lambda return values may be arrays and must participate in deterministic spill tiling/broadcast output.\n- `INDIRECT` supports both A1 and R1C1 text references.\n- `RAND`/`RANDARRAY` outputs remain within bounds and change via small perturbations on explicit recalculation events.\n\n### 3.5 Recalc and Epoch Model\n- `committed_epoch`, `stabilized_epoch`, and per-value `value_epoch`.\n- `Automatic` and `Manual` recalc modes.\n- Internal recalculation strategy is non-normative (dependency graph/tree shape, scheduling, incremental vs full recompute are implementation choices).\n- Normative requirement is externally observable behavior: deterministic stabilized outputs and correct epoch/staleness semantics for identical input + API-call sequences.\n\n### 3.6 Structural Rewrite Path (Required)\n- Row/column structural mutations are in-scope:\n  - `insert_row`, `delete_row`, `insert_col`, `delete_col`.\n- Formula/name references are rewritten deterministically.\n- Invalidated references are surfaced explicitly (for example `#REF!` behavior).\n- Mixed and absolute references must preserve anchoring flags through rewrites; coordinate rewriting under structural edits applies to all reference classes (`A1`, `$A1`, `A$1`, `$A$1`).\n- Structural mutation requests use a tri-state outcome model:\n  - `Applied`: mutation accepted and committed.\n  - `Rejected`: request is valid but cannot be executed due to structural/policy constraints.\n  - `Invalid`: request is malformed or out of contract.\n- Rejected structural requests are atomic no-ops:\n  - no partial mutation,\n  - no `committed_epoch` increment,\n  - deterministic, user-visible rejection reason.\n\n### 3.7 Iteration and Cycle Handling\n- SCC cycle detection remains deterministic.\n- Engine supports iterative cycle mode via iteration configuration (`enabled`, max iterations, convergence tolerance).\n- When iteration is disabled, circular references follow Excel-style non-iterative behavior:\n  - no hard recalculation failure solely due to circularity,\n  - circular paths read prior stabilized values when available, otherwise `0.0`.\n- Cycle detection is also an observable signal:\n  - each recalculation that detects circularity in non-iterative mode emits at least one non-fatal diagnostic notification (for host/UI feedback parity with Excel warning behavior).\n\n### 3.8 Volatility and Invalidation Classes\n- Functions/UDFs are classified as:\n  - `Standard`,\n  - `Volatile`,\n  - `ExternallyInvalidated`.\n- Volatile refresh and externally-triggered refresh are separate pathways (`invalidate_volatile`, stream ticks, `invalidate_udf`).\n- Volatile formulas do not self-tick in the background; they update when recalculation/invalidation is explicitly triggered.\n\n### 3.9 External UDFs\n- External UDF registration/unregistration is in scope.\n- UDF volatility class participates in invalidation behavior.\n\n### 3.10 Engine Entities Beyond Cells/Names\n- Controls are engine-managed named-value entities with metadata.\n- Charts are engine-managed sink entities producing computed chart outputs.\n- Change tracking is engine-managed via opt-in journal/drain API.\n\n### 3.11 Formatting\n- Per-cell metadata formatting is in scope:\n  - decimals,\n  - bold/italic,\n  - foreground/background palette colors.\n- Formatting does not change formula semantics.\n\n### 3.12 API-Visible Invariants and Conformance\n- API-visible invariants are part of the normative contract for compatibility claims.\n- Initial invariant and case registry is defined in `docs/ENGINE_CONFORMANCE_TESTS.md`.\n- Property forms and temporal semantics are defined in `docs/ENGINE_FORMAL_PROPERTIES.md`.\n- Implementations claiming compatibility should report conformance outcomes against that registry.\n\n## 4. Non-goals (Round 0)\n- Multi-sheet workbook semantics.\n- OOXML fidelity and full Excel object model compatibility.\n- Collaboration/replication protocols.\n- VBA runtime hosting.\n- Full XLL/COM parity.\n\n## 5. References\n- `docs/ENGINE_REQUIREMENTS.md`\n- `docs/ENGINE_API.md`\n- `docs/ENGINE_CONFORMANCE_TESTS.md`\n- `docs/ENGINE_FORMAL_PROPERTIES.md`\n- `docs/SPEC_v0_INTEGRATION_APPENDIX.md`\n\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}} {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_34","type":"command_execution","command":"\"C:\\\\Program Files\\\\PowerShell\\\\7\\\\pwsh.exe\" -Command 'Get-Content -Raw \"prompts/runs/20260308-171858-core-engine-fec-f3e-deep-research-pack-01/inputs/source/fec-f3e-evidence__EXAM_SUMMARY.md\"'","aggregated_output":"# FEC/F3E Redesign Examination Summary (2026-03-08)\n\n## Scope\nRecorded transactional-seam traces for:\n1. `seam_dynamic_reference_retargeting_flows`\n2. `seam_spill_takeover_and_clearance_on_referenced_spill_child`\n\n## Artifacts\n- `dynamic_retargeting_trace.log`\n- `dynamic_retargeting_trace.event_counts.tsv`\n- `dynamic_retargeting_trace.callgraph.edges.csv`\n- `dynamic_retargeting_trace.callgraph.dot`\n- `spill_takeover_clearance_trace.log`\n- `spill_takeover_clearance_trace.event_counts.tsv`\n- `spill_takeover_clearance_trace.callgraph.edges.csv`\n- `spill_takeover_clearance_trace.callgraph.dot`\n\n## Event Volume\n- dynamic retargeting flow: `65` boundary events\n- spill takeover/clearance flow: `51` boundary events\n\n## Key Observations\n1. Dynamic retargeting emits dependency deltas during selector path transitions.\n   - `D1` and `D2` commits showed `dep_delta_cells>0` when runtime targets changed.\n2. Spill-shape transitions are explicit at commit.\n   - `spill_shape_delta=created` when spill expanded.\n   - `spill_shape_delta=cleared` when spill shrank away.\n3. Transaction statuses were deterministic in these runs.\n   - all observed commits were `status=Applied`.\n4. Call-graph phase pattern is stable:\n   - `fec.open_session -> fec.capability_view -> f3e.execute -> fec.commit -> engine.evaluate_cell_via_f3e`\n\n## Practical Impact\n1. Dynamic `INDIRECT`/`OFFSET` targets now participate in runtime dependency tracking, improving incremental invalidation behavior.\n2. Spill takeover/clearance transitions are now surfaced as seam metadata rather than implicit side effects.\n\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}} {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_35","type":"agent_message","text":"I’ve got the governing sources loaded and the main architecture seams are clear enough to start synthesis. 3: Working docs for the DNA Calc program foundation and planning.\r\n   4: DNA Calc is developed by DNA Kode.\r\n   5: \r\n   6: Start with `CHARTER.md` for mission/doctrine, then `ARCHITECTURE_AND_REQUIREMENTS.md` for the system shape and constraints.\r\n   7: Use `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` for ongoing core formal/semantic model work and extraction of formal details from architecture docs.\r\n   8: Use `OPERATIONS.md` for team/process mechanics, `notes/BRAINSTORM_NOTES.md` for captured ideas, and `prompts/` for reusable prompt packs.\r\n   9: Use `notes/RUNNING_PROJECT_NOTES.md` for dated ongoing idea/to-do capture.\r\n  10: Use `notes/RESEARCH_NOTES.md` for synthesized retained research knowledge.\r\n  11: Use `notes/VISICALC_V0_SCOPE_ALIGNMENT_NOTES.md` for retained v0 scope-alignment notes and deferred expansion backlog from superseded pathfinder guidance docs.\r\n  12: For DnaVisiCalc Round 0 functional scope, treat `..\\\\DnaVisiCalc\\\\docs\\\\SPEC_v0.md`, `..\\\\DnaVisiCalc\\\\docs\\\\ENGINE_REQUIREMENTS.md`, and `..\\\\DnaVisiCalc\\\\docs\\\\ENGINE_API.md` as authoritative.\r\n  13: Use Foundation docs to hold doctrine, architecture framing, and process that remain consistent with that upstream scope.\r\n  14: See `prompts/README.md` for prompt execution guidance and run artifacts.\r\n  15: See `research/README.md` for topic/source registry and deep-research run artifacts.\r\n  16: See `reference/README.md` for reference-spec mirror, managed processing runs, and curated empirical conformance findings.\r\n  17: Active OxFunc function/value working docs now live in `..\\OxFunc\\docs\\function-lane\\`; Foundation keeps the Excel reference/spec corpus under `reference\\`.\r\n  18: See `REFERENCE_SPEC_FORMAT_AND_CONFORMANCE.md` for the normalized reference/conformance artifact contract.\r\n  19: See `tools/README.md` for local tooling layout and policy notes.\r\n  20: See `synthesis/README.md` for synthesis-pass workflow and decision logging.\r\n\r\n=== CHARTER.md ===\r\n   1: # CHARTER.md — DNA Calc Charter\r\n   2: \r\n   3: ## 1. Mission\r\n   4: **DNA Calc Mission**  \r\n   5: DNA Calc is a near-formal, state-of-the-art spreadsheet platform that matches Excel’s behavior with high fidelity while remaining rigorously specified, verifiable, and built to evolve. It is a clean-room implementation based only on public documentation, published research, and reproducible observation of Excel’s behavior—never proprietary sources or reverse-engineering of internals. DNA Calc prioritizes correctness under concurrency, graceful degradation across versions and profiles, and stable, versioned interfaces that support a long-lived ecosystem of tools, add-ins, and automation.\r\n   6: \r\n   7: ## 2. Doctrine\r\n   8: Doctrine is mandatory operating guidance. **Hygiene** is listed before **Evolution**.\r\n   9: \r\n  10: ### 2.1 Hygiene Doctrine\r\n  11: 1. **Spec-first (or spec-with) changes**  \r\n  12:    Behavior changes are not “done” until spec + cases are updated and gates pass.\r\n  13: 2. **Computed obligations, no hand-waving**  \r\n  14:    Required checks are determined mechanically from diffs and profiles; bypass is rare and explicit.\r\n  15: 3. **One-command readiness**  \r\n  16:    `meta check` (or equivalent) is the standard way to declare a state “green.”\r\n  17: 4. **Regressions are assets**  \r\n  18:    Every bug becomes a minimized, machine-replayable trace/case artifact and remains in the corpus.\r\n  19: 5. **Determinism-first debugging**  \r\n  20:    Deterministic modes exist for triage and conformance runs; non-determinism is opt-in and labeled.\r\n  21: 6. **Evidence discipline**  \r\n  22:    Every Excel-compatibility claim is backed by public sources and/or a reproducible observation harness.\r\n  23: 7. **Coupled assurance stack**  \r\n  24:    Claims of correctness must stay coupled across semantics, proofs/models, executable tests, and CI gate artifacts.\r\n  25: \r\n  26: ### 2.2 Evolution Doctrine\r\n  27: 1. **Profiles are the semantics spine**  \r\n  28:    Documents bind to `profile_id` + `profile_version`. Breaking semantic changes require profile bumps.\r\n  29: 2. **Protocols are versioned and negotiated**  \r\n  30:    OpLog, engine protocol surface, automation protocol, UI protocol—each has explicit versions and negotiation.\r\n  31: 3. **Degrade gracefully; never crash**  \r\n  32:    Unsupported or unknown features must preserve data (opaque) and/or surface explicit deterministic errors/warnings.\r\n  33: 4. **Unknown parts round-trip**  \r\n  34:    When feasible, unrecognized file parts and extension payloads are preserved byte-for-byte.\r\n  35: 5. **Migrations are explicit and replayable**  \r\n  36:    Migrations are expressed as operations and are testable/replayable.\r\n  37: 6. **No hidden mutation pathways**  \r\n  38:    All persistent state changes flow through the operation model; no “special cases” for UI, scripts, VBA, XLL, or collaboration.\r\n  39: \r\n  40: ### 2.3 Named Program Principles\r\n  41: 1. **Alien Artifact leverage**\r\n  42:    Use mathematically strong methods when they remove ambiguity and increase confidence, but keep each method tied to executable invariants and artifacts.\r\n  43: 2. **Design for Evolution**\r\n  44:    Keep seams explicit, versioned, and negotiable so behavior can expand without semantic drift or silent breakage.\r\n  45: \r\n  46: ## 3. Program Structure and Names\r\n  47: ### 3.1 System Family\r\n  48: - **DNA Calc** (code token: `DnaCalc`) — the Goldilocks long-term foundation.\r\n  49: - **DNA VisiCalc** (`DnaVisiCalc`) — Round 0 pathfinder.  \r\n  50:   Functional scope authority for Round 0 is maintained in the DnaVisiCalc docs set (`SPEC_v0.md`, `ENGINE_REQUIREMENTS.md`, `ENGINE_API.md`); Foundation doctrine/process docs must remain consistent with that scope.\r\n  51: - **DNA PreCalc** (`DnaPreCalc`) — Round 1 full end-to-end.\r\n  52: - **DNA SuperCalc** (`DnaSuperCalc`) — Round 2 refactor/perfection pass.\r\n  53: - **DNA Calc** (`DnaCalc`) — Round 3 synthesized long-term product.\r\n  54: \r\n  55: ### 3.2 Team Colors\r\n  56: - **Green** — Spec stack + verification: DSLs, Lean proofs, TLA+ models, OCaml oracle, conformance packs.\r\n  57: - **Red** — Rust implementation.\r\n  58: - **Blue** — .NET implementation.\r\n  59: \r\n  60: (“Logistics” exists as a function; it may be implemented primarily in .NET tooling.)\r\n  61: \r\n  62: ## 4. Clean-room Rule (Non-negotiable)\r\n  63: DNA Calc development relies only on:\r\n  64: - public specifications and documentation,\r\n  65: - published research,\r\n  66: - reproducible observation of Excel behavior.\r\n  67: \r\n  68: Excluded:\r\n  69: - proprietary code, restricted materials, decompilation/disassembly of Excel internals, or reverse engineering of internals.\r\n  70: \r\n  71: ## 5. Glossary (short)\r\n  72: - **Profile**: A versioned semantics bundle defining meaning, compatibility rules, and required obligation packs.\r\n  73: - **OpLog**: The operation log—the single representation of persistent state changes.\r\n  74: - **Epoch / Meta-epoch**: Epoch versions document state; meta-epochs version stabilized project states (profiles + packs + implementations).\r\n  75: - **Calc stabilized**: A runtime/data state where derived outputs are complete for a declared scope and epoch.\r\n  76: - **Profile green**: A release/readiness state where all required obligation packs for a profile are passing.\r\n  77: - **Stabilized epoch**: The latest epoch whose derived values are complete for a declared scope.\r\n  78: - **Meta-epoch commit**: A published stabilization checkpoint containing capability manifest, conformance report, and regression updates.\r\n  79: - **Artifact freeze**: A required set of versioned artifacts that must be locked before advancing rounds or declaring a profile green.\r\n  80: - **External update op**: An explicit OpLog operation representing inbound STREAM/RTD-like value changes.\r\n  81: - **Stream semantics version**: A profile-scoped version token controlling STREAM/external update behavior.\r\n  82: - **Stream replay bundle**: A versioned artifact containing topic declarations and ordered update envelopes for deterministic replay.\r\n  83: - **Degradation class**: Policy outcome for unsupported behavior (`Native`, `Lowered`, `Opaque`, `Rejected`).\r\n  84: - **Obligation pack**: A computed set of checks required to claim readiness for a profile.\r\n  85: - **Capability manifest**: Runtime/Build metadata describing supported protocols, profiles, features, and pack results.\r\n\r\n=== ARCHITECTURE_AND_REQUIREMENTS.md ===\r\n   1: # ARCHITECTURE_AND_REQUIREMENTS.md — DNA Calc Architecture and Requirements\r\n   2: \r\n   3: ## 1. Overview\r\n   4: DNA Calc is a near-formal spreadsheet system with two independent engines (Rust and .NET) sharing identical protocol surfaces and validated against a Green-owned spec stack: Lean (semantics proofs), TLA+ (concurrency protocol checks), OCaml oracle (executable reference), and conformance packs.\r\n   5: \r\n   6: ### 1.1 Three Hard Boundaries (core architectural shape)\r\n   7: 1. **OpLog (Operations)**\r\n   8:    - All persistent state changes are operations (including structural edits, external updates, macro edits).\r\n   9: 2. **DocSnapshot (Versioned Document State)**\r\n  10:    - Immutable snapshots per epoch/meta-epoch. Inputs are truth; derived values are caches.\r\n  11: 3. **CalcDeltas (Derived Outputs)**\r\n  12:    - Engine produces deltas tagged with version info (epoch/value_epoch) and explicit stale/pending status.\r\n  13: \r\n  14: ## 2. Requirements Taxonomy (how to write requirements)\r\n  15: ### 2.1 Architecture-independent requirements (REQ-)\r\n  16: Observable behaviors and quality targets, independent of internal mechanisms.\r\n  17: \r\n  18: ### 2.2 Architecture-dependent constraints (CONSTR-)\r\n  19: Enforceable structural rules derived from Mission/Doctrine.\r\n  20: \r\n  21: ### 2.3 Architecture-anchored intents and realizations (INT-/REAL-)\r\n  22: - **Intent (INT-)**: desired outcome, mechanism-agnostic.\r\n  23: - **Realization (REAL-)**: precise, testable specification anchored to chosen architecture.\r\n  24: \r\n  25: ## 3. System Architecture (A1)\r\n  26: This section now includes a formal-core layer model intended to be shared by Green proofs/models, the OCaml oracle, and both delivery engines.\r\n  27: Detailed core formal/semantic model development is maintained in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md`.\r\n  28: This document should keep architecture-level summaries and stable constraints, with minimal duplication of formal-core detail.\r\n  29: \r\n  30: ### 3.1 Protocol Surface (identical across Red/Blue)\r\n  31: - Dispatch ops/transactions.\r\n  32: - Query snapshots/state.\r\n  33: - Subscribe to deltas/events.\r\n  34: - Capability negotiation.\r\n  35: \r\n  36: Detailed core semantics are in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md`:\r\n  37: - protocol baseline: Section `5.1`,\r\n  38: - operation/replay semantics: Section `6.4`.\r\n  39: \r\n  40: ### 3.2 Profiles, Feature Gates, and Compatibility\r\n  41: - Profiles remain the semantic spine (`profile_id` + `profile_version`) for compatibility, feature gates, obligation packs, and degrade behavior.\r\n  42: \r\n  43: Detailed core profile semantics are in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` Section `5.2`.\r\n  44: \r\n  45: ### 3.3 Epoch Model (MVCC-style)\r\n  46: - Core epoch semantics (`committed/stabilized/value epoch`, stale/pending visibility, pinning/GC constraints) remain mandatory.\r\n  47: - Core calc-delta derived-output semantics remain mandatory.\r\n  48: \r\n  49: Detailed definitions are in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` Section `5.3`.\r\n  50: \r\n  51: ### 3.4 Calculation Engine Pipeline (conceptual)\r\n  52: - Core pipeline remains parse/bind/dependency/invalidation/schedule/evaluate/commit with deterministic and incremental requirements.\r\n  53: - `NodeId`-based dependency identity remains baseline.\r\n  54: - Both invariant-oriented and dirty-closure propagation models remain valid formal baselines.\r\n  55: \r\n  56: Detailed definitions are in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` Section `5.4`.\r\n  57: \r\n  58: ### 3.5 External Streaming and RTD-like Behavior\r\n  59: - Pathfinder: `STREAM(\"topic\")` is acceptable and deterministic (epoch-scoped external provider).\r\n  60: - Full system: RTD support (topic lifecycle, updates, invalidations) is a core interop feature.\r\n  61: - External updates must appear as explicit `OpExternalUpdate` ops (`topic_id`, `topic_seq`, payload ref/envelope) and be replayable for test harnesses where required.\r\n  62: - STREAM/external update semantics include explicit topic identity, dedupe rules, ordering guarantees, and coalescing policy.\r\n  63: - Profile policy defines whether oracle values are local-only or shared for collaboration scenarios.\r\n  64: - A stream replay bundle (topic declarations, updates, timing/order envelope) is a required artifact for conformance and minimization.\r\n  65: \r\n  66: #### 3.5.1 External Invalidation vs Volatile Invalidation\r\n  67: - External functions (`STREAM`, RTD, and profile-marked externally-invalidated UDFs) recalculate on explicit external signal, not on every volatile cycle.\r\n  68: - Volatile functions recalculate on invalidation cycles triggered by host policy.\r\n  69: - Distinct invalidation pathways are required:\r\n  70:   - volatile invalidation scope (global or class-based),\r\n  71:   - external invalidation scope (targeted by provider/topic/UDF identity).\r\n  72: - Both pathways converge on the same dirty-closure propagation and deterministic evaluation pipeline.\r\n  73: \r\n  74: ### 3.6 External UDFs / XLL-like integration\r\n  75: Pathfinder scope includes:\r\n  76: - external UDF registration/unregistration with explicit volatility class,\r\n  77: - externally driven invalidation hooks (`invalidate_udf`) that feed normal dirty-closure propagation,\r\n  78: - deterministic caller-driven execution (no autonomous internal timers/threads in pathfinder engine boundary),\r\n  79: - UDFs treated as pure-oracle from Lean/TLA+ perspective (semantics parameterized by oracle results).\r\n  80: \r\n  81: Volatility classification:\r\n  82: - `Standard`: recalculates when upstream dependencies change.\r\n  83: - `Volatile`: recalculates on host invalidation cycle.\r\n  84: - `ExternallyInvalidated`: recalculates on explicit external signal.\r\n  85: \r\n  86: Built-in and UDF volatility classification is profile-governed and must be reflected in capability/profile artifacts.\r\n  87: \r\n  88: Full system adds:\r\n  89: - full XLL compatibility including marshalling/lifetime contracts and RTD integration.\r\n  90: - XLL is in-process with the engine; boundary contracts are formally specified and validated by Green packs.\r\n  91: \r\n  92: ### 3.6.1 Controls and Charts as Engine Entities\r\n  93: - Controls/charts are core engine entities participating in dependency semantics and operation/replay lifecycle.\r\n  94: \r\n  95: Detailed core semantics are in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` Section `5.6`.\r\n  96: \r\n  97: ### 3.7 VBA and Macros (outside core)\r\n  98: - VBA runtime and editor live outside the core engine.\r\n  99: - Core engine stores the VBA project as a document object:\r\n 100:   - opaque blob (e.g., `vbaProject.bin`) + minimal metadata.\r\n 101: - Application layer glues:\r\n 102:   - file I/O ↔ engine (store/retrieve blob),\r\n 103:   - VBA runtime ↔ engine (macros emit ops via protocol),\r\n 104:   - macro execution occurs in an exclusive mutation mode (serialized event stream).\r\n 105: \r\n 106: Windows-only COM automation is a separate facade layered on top of the identical protocol surface.\r\n 107: \r\n 108: ### 3.8 File I/O (outside core, full fidelity)\r\n 109: - File adapters are external components that translate to/from the object model and ops.\r\n 110: - For Excel interop:\r\n 111:   - preserve unknown/unsupported OOXML parts where feasible (opaque attachments),\r\n 112:   - never silently drop meaning on round-trip,\r\n 113:   - lowering pipeline may translate internal constructs to Excel-safe constructs or explicit loss markers,\r\n 114:   - degrade outcomes must be surfaced through diagnostics with policy class (`Native` / `Lowered` / `Opaque` / `Rejected`).\r\n 115: \r\n 116: ### 3.9 Collaboration (designed-in seam)\r\n 117: - Collaboration modeled as replication of the OpLog.\r\n 118: - Initial design preference: server-sequenced ops (deterministic shared log).\r\n 119: - Identity under structural edits is considered early (stable IDs where needed).\r\n 120: - Derived calc is generally local; external oracles (RTD) may be local or shared depending on profile policy.\r\n 121: - Replication envelope requires operation idempotency, causal ordering metadata, and transaction grouping.\r\n 122: \r\n 123: ### 3.10 UI Architecture (intended stack)\r\n 124: - Tauri shell with web UI.\r\n 125: - Grid rendering:\r\n 126:   - Canvas/WebGL for the giant grid (virtualized; no DOM-per-cell).\r\n 127:   - DOM overlay editor for Excel-grade editing (IME, selection, clipboard).\r\n 128: - UI state machine (“reducer” style) with explicit modes (selecting, editing, formula ref picking, fill, resize).\r\n 129: - Geometry spec and hit-test invariants.\r\n 130: - RenderPlan IR used for deterministic testing (avoid screenshot brittleness).\r\n 131: - View state is partially document-backed (saved view settings) and partially session state.\r\n 132: - UI reliability requires property-level invariants for geometry/hit-test consistency and deterministic RenderPlan validation.\r\n 133: \r\n 134: ### 3.10.1 Core and Non-core Interaction Summary\r\n 135: - UI/TUI layers read snapshots/deltas and dispatch explicit operations; no hidden persistent core mutation path is allowed.\r\n 136: - File adapters translate external formats to/from core entities and operations, and surface degradation outcomes explicitly.\r\n 137: - VBA/macro runtimes execute outside core and interact through operation pathways.\r\n 138: - Collaboration adapters replicate operation envelopes and preserve causality/idempotency constraints.\r\n 139: \r\n 140: Formal-model note:\r\n 141: - Detailed formal semantics for the core engine model are in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md`.\r\n 142: - Sections `3.11`..`3.17` in this document are architecture summaries linked to that detailed model.\r\n 143: \r\n 144: ### 3.11 Formal State Kernel (tree-grid hybrid with persistence facades)\r\n 145: Detailed model is in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` (`6.1`).\r\n 146: Architecture summary:\r\n 147: - immutable green core + ephemeral red facade split,\r\n 148: - identity is ID-based (not coordinate-string-based),\r\n 149: - representation strategy may vary while preserving identical semantics.\r\n 150: \r\n 151: ### 3.12 Layered Semantics (structure, refs, deps, values, ops)\r\n 152: Detailed model is in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` (`6.2`).\r\n 153: Architecture summary:\r\n 154: - five-layer model `S/R/D/V/O`,\r\n 155: - explicit derivation contracts between layers,\r\n 156: - mutation authority remains operation-driven.\r\n 157: \r\n 158: ### 3.13 OpLog Formal Transition Semantics\r\n 159: Detailed model is in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` (`6.4`).\r\n 160: Architecture summary:\r\n 161: - `OpEnvelope` is the canonical persistent-change carrier,\r\n 162: - `apply_op` is the canonical transition relation,\r\n 163: - replay target is observational equivalence under profile constraints.\r\n 164: \r\n 165: ### 3.14 Structural Rewrite Semantics (rows/cols/sheets)\r\n 166: Detailed model is in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` (`6.5`).\r\n 167: Architecture summary:\r\n 168: - structural edits require deterministic rewrite functions and reference-classification outputs,\r\n 169: - invalidated targets must remain explicit and traceable.\r\n 170: \r\n 171: ### 3.15 Reference Resolution and Reference-Grid Update Semantics\r\n 172: Detailed model is in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` (`6.3`).\r\n 173: Architecture summary:\r\n 174: - normalized reference forms are required for deterministic binder outputs,\r\n 175: - reference-grid updates must preserve explicit forward/reverse/provenance information,\r\n 176: - unresolved references remain explicit errors, never silent drops.\r\n 177: \r\n 178: ### 3.16 Cycle Detection, Iteration, and Stabilization Semantics\r\n 179: Detailed model is in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` (`6.6`).\r\n 180: Architecture summary:\r\n 181: - SCC processing order is deterministic,\r\n 182: - cycle mode is profile-governed (`CycleError` vs `Iterative`),\r\n 183: - stabilization rules are explicit and terminal-state based.\r\n 184: \r\n 185: ### 3.17 Formalization Seams for Lean and OCaml\r\n 186: Detailed seam definitions are in `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` (`6.7`).\r\n 187: Architecture summary:\r\n 188: - Lean, OCaml oracle, and shared trace schemas remain mandatory integration seams,\r\n 189: - the `CoreIds/CoreStructure/CoreRefs/CoreDeps/CoreEval/CoreOps` split remains baseline.\r\n 190: \r\n 191: ### 3.18 FEC/F3E Lane Boundary (OxFml/OxFunc/FEC)\r\n 192: To reduce ownership drift during implementation spikes, lane ownership is explicitly split as follows:\r\n 193: - **OxFml** owns formula grammar, parse, and bind semantics.\r\n 194: - **OxFunc** owns value-type and function semantics consumed during evaluation.\r\n 195: - **FEC host/model lane** owns host protocol, capability policy, dependency lifecycle, scheduler interaction, and publication lifecycle.\r\n 196: \r\n 197: Current working references:\r\n 198: - `reference/conformance/excel-worksheet-engine/model/EXCEL_FORMULA_EVALUATION_CONTEXT_FEC.md`\r\n 199: - `reference/conformance/excel-worksheet-engine/model/FEC_F3E_INTERFACE_DRAFT_SPEC.md`\r\n 200: - `reference/conformance/excel-worksheet-engine/model/FEC_F3E_PROTOCOL_CONFORMANCE_MATRIX.csv`\r\n 201: \r\n 202: ## 4. Architectural Constraints (A2 / CONSTR- examples)\r\n 203: - **CONSTR-001:** All persistent mutations are ops; direct document mutation is forbidden outside the coordinator.\r\n 204: - **CONSTR-002:** File and network I/O are adapters outside core; core engine has no socket/file dependencies.\r\n 205: - **CONSTR-003:** Unsupported constructs never crash; they preserve or degrade explicitly.\r\n 206: - **CONSTR-004:** Protocol surfaces are identical across Red/Blue; compatibility negotiation is mandatory.\r\n 207: - **CONSTR-005:** Deterministic mode must exist and be used for conformance and minimization runs.\r\n 208: - **CONSTR-006:** Spec stack/oracle/tool integration contracts are file/CLI-based with schema-versioned artifacts.\r\n 209: - **CONSTR-007:** Compatibility claims require linked clean-room evidence records tied to REQ/INT/REAL identifiers.\r\n 210: - **CONSTR-008:** Engine lock discipline forbids awaiting or user-callback execution while holding mutation-critical locks.\r\n 211: - **CONSTR-009:** Performance readiness uses deterministic phase counters and published scaling signatures per required profile.\r\n 212: - **CONSTR-010:** Snapshot identity is ID-based (`RowId`/`ColId`/`CellId`), not coordinate-string-based; address projection is derived.\r\n 213: - **CONSTR-011:** Every structural op must define deterministic axis rewrite functions and explicit rewrite classification for affected references.\r\n 214: - **CONSTR-012:** Reference layer must model region nodes and error references explicitly; unresolved references cannot be silently discarded.\r\n 215: - **CONSTR-013:** Cycle handling mode (`CycleError` or `Iterative`) is profile-defined and deterministic with explicit terminal behavior.\r\n 216: - **CONSTR-014:** Op envelopes require idempotency/causality metadata sufficient for deterministic replay and replication safety.\r\n 217: - **CONSTR-015:** Green/Red persistence-facade split must preserve immutable core semantics and avoid hidden mutation in facade caches.\r\n 218: - **CONSTR-016:** Function invalidation classes (`Standard` / `Volatile` / `ExternallyInvalidated`) must have deterministic, non-ambiguous trigger semantics per profile.\r\n 219: - **CONSTR-017:** Control/chart lifecycle mutations must be represented as explicit operations and replay artifacts, never as UI-only hidden state.\r\n 220: \r\n\r\n=== OPERATIONS.md ===\r\n   1: # OPERATIONS.md — DNA Calc Operations\r\n   2: \r\n   3: ## 1. Purpose\r\n   4: Operations defines how DNA Calc is developed, stabilized, and evolved under the Mission and Doctrine. It is designed to withstand “agentic coding weather” and frequent plan changes.\r\n   5: \r\n   6: ## 2. Structure (Green/Red/Blue) and Veto\r\n   7: ### 2.1 Responsibilities\r\n   8: **Green (Spec & Assurance)**\r\n   9: - Owns DSL specs, proofs, protocol specs, OCaml oracle, cases, conformance packs.\r\n  10: - Owns profile definitions, profile versioning rules, and negotiation schemas.\r\n  11: - Owns `CORE_ENGINE_FORMAL_MODEL.md` as the detailed formal-semantics working document.\r\n  12: - Curates minimized regressions and “known quirks” documentation.\r\n  13: - **Has veto on declaring a profile “stabilized/green.”**\r\n  14: \r\n  15: **Red (Rust engine)**\r\n  16: - Implements the protocol surface and passes Green’s packs.\r\n  17: - Focuses on hard-rock correctness under concurrency and performance scaling.\r\n  18: \r\n  19: **Blue (.NET engine)**\r\n  20: - Implements the identical protocol surface and passes Green’s packs.\r\n  21: - Provides independent confirmation of spec clarity and implementation feasibility.\r\n  22: \r\n  23: **Logistics (Tooling & Coordination)**\r\n  24: - Builds and maintains `meta` orchestration, CI wiring, artifact storage/reporting, dashboards.\r\n  25: - Keeps cycle time low and reproducibility high.\r\n  26: - Generally .NET-first tooling, with small Rust utilities when warranted.\r\n  27: \r\n  28: ### 2.2 Module Triads\r\n  29: Every major spec/module area is managed by a **triad**:\r\n  30: - **Design** representative (spec intent and structure),\r\n  31: - **Assurance** representative (packs, proofs, test strategy),\r\n  32: - **Delivery** representative (Red/Blue implementation implications).\r\n  33: \r\n  34: Triads resolve ambiguity early and ensure artifacts remain consistent.\r\n  35: \r\n  36: ## 3. Development Recalc Cycle (mirrors calculation)\r\n  37: The project evolves through a “recalc” cycle operating on DAGs.\r\n  38: \r\n  39: ### 3.1 DAGs\r\n  40: - **Spec DAG**: Lean modules, TLA+ modules, schemas/IDL, profiles.\r\n  41: - **Assurance DAG**: obligation packs, oracle runs, minimized cases, perf signatures.\r\n  42: - **Implementation DAG**: Red modules, Blue modules, adapters, protocol surfaces.\r\n  43: - **Interop DAG** (later/heavier): Excel differential packs, file adapter conformance.\r\n  44: \r\n  45: ### 3.2 Phase Model\r\n  46: 1. **Edit / Dirty Marking**\r\n  47:    - Any change marks a set of nodes dirty (modules, packs, profiles, protocols), including `OpExternalUpdate`.\r\n  48: 2. **Dependency Closure**\r\n  49:    - `meta` computes impacted obligations and required version bumps.\r\n  50: 3. **Scheduling**\r\n  51:    - Work is dispatched along parallel tracks: Design / Assurance / Delivery / Logistics.\r\n  52: 4. **Execution**\r\n  53:    - Proofs/model checks, oracle runs, engine conformance, perf signatures.\r\n  54:    - For behavior-sensitive changes, execute a coupled evidence lane: semantics/spec delta, proof/model output, deterministic replay artifacts, and scaling signature evidence.\r\n  55: 5. **Stabilization**\r\n  56:    - A profile is stabilized when its required obligation packs pass and artifacts are emitted.\r\n  57: 6. **Meta-epoch Commit**\r\n  58:    - Publish capability manifest + conformance report + regression updates.\r\n  59: \r\n  60: ### 3.3 Sequence-Only Planning Doctrine (No Time Schedules)\r\n  61: Project execution planning is sequence-based, not calendar-based.\r\n  62: \r\n  63: Mandatory rule:\r\n  64: 1. Plans and execution notes must not use date-based or duration-based commitments (for example deadlines, ETA dates, week-based schedules, or \"finish by X time\").\r\n  65: 2. Execution order must be expressed using:\r\n  66:    - priority,\r\n  67:    - dependency/`depends_on` relations,\r\n  68:    - blocker status,\r\n  69:    - gate/pack readiness criteria,\r\n  70:    - risk and impact ordering.\r\n  71: 3. Progress is reported by state transitions and obligation closure (for example `planned -> in_progress -> blocked -> complete`), not by elapsed time targets.\r\n  72: 4. If temporal metadata is captured for audit/provenance (timestamps in artifacts), it is observational only and must not be used as a planning commitment axis.\r\n  73: \r\n  74: ### 3.4 Auto vs Manual Modes (dev analogy)\r\n  75: - **Auto**: CI runs the full required packs for affected profiles.\r\n  76: - **Manual**: local development may defer heavy packs, but merge requires stabilization.\r\n  77: \r\n  78: ## 4. Obligation Packs and Gates\r\n  79: ### 4.1 Packs\r\n  80: Packs are the unit of readiness (examples):\r\n  81: - `PACK.visicalc.core` (Pathfinder semantics)\r\n  82: - `PACK.concurrent.epochs` (TLA+ invariants and schedule tests)\r\n  83: - `PACK.udf.basic` (external UDF registration + calls)\r\n  84: - `PACK.lean.ocaml.alignment.core` (Lean-bounded fixtures aligned with OCaml oracle outcomes)\r\n  85: - `PACK.stream.basic` (STREAM/external updates propagation)\r\n  86: - `PACK.stream.oracle.diff` (cross-engine + oracle stream replay parity)\r\n  87: - `PACK.structural.insert` (insert row/col rewrite + traces)\r\n  88: - `PACK.ui.viewport` (geometry/hit-test invariants + RenderPlan checks)\r\n  89: - `PACK.scaling.signature` (growth suite and slope reporting)\r\n  90: - `PACK.interop.degrade_matrix` (Native/Lowered/Opaque/Rejected policy conformance)\r\n  91: - `PACK.interop.roundtrip.opaque` (unknown-part round-trip guarantees)\r\n  92: - `PACK.collab.replication.core` (OpLog replication envelope and idempotency checks)\r\n  93: \r\n  94: Additional Round 1 candidate packs informed by pathfinder evidence:\r\n  95: - `PACK.control.basic` (control definition, validation, dependency participation)\r\n  96: - `PACK.chart.basic` (chart definition, sink-node evaluation, chart-output determinism)\r\n  97: - `PACK.calcdelta.basic` (typed delta entries, epoch tagging, emission/drain semantics)\r\n  98: - `PACK.volatility.three_cat` (Standard/Volatile/ExternallyInvalidated invalidation behavior)\r\n  99: \r\n 100: Pack status terminology:\r\n 101: - `exercised`: implementation-level behavior exists with local tests.\r\n 102: - `green-validated`: Green-owned pack artifacts and required conformance evidence are complete.\r\n 103: - `exercised` is not sufficient for stabilization claims.\r\n 104: \r\n 105: ### 4.2 Gate Rules\r\n 106: - A profile cannot be declared “stabilized” unless all required packs for that profile are green.\r\n 107: - Failing packs must generate or update minimized cases.\r\n 108: - Required packs for a claim must include triangulation evidence across OCaml oracle, Red (Rust), and Blue (.NET) where applicable.\r\n 109: - STREAM readiness for Round 0 requires `PACK.stream.basic` and stream cases in `PACK.concurrent.epochs`.\r\n 110: \r\n 111: ### 4.3 Pack Contract Discipline\r\n 112: - Every pack publishes:\r\n 113:   - scope, required fixtures, deterministic mode requirements, pass/fail thresholds, emitted artifacts.\r\n 114: - `PACK.scaling.signature` contract must include:\r\n 115:   - required workload families, phase-level counter schema, slope calculation method, regression thresholds.\r\n 116: - `PACK.concurrent.epochs` contract must include:\r\n 117:   - tiered model-check configurations and archived minimized counterexample traces.\r\n 118: - `PACK.visicalc.core` contract must include:\r\n 119:   - minimum semantic coverage and required artifact set for Round 0 profile readiness.\r\n 120: \r\n 121: ## 5. Regression Handling (AAR-driven)\r\n 122: - Every failure produces:\r\n 123:   - a minimized trace,\r\n 124:   - a conformance report entry,\r\n 125:   - a triage record (root cause classification).\r\n 126: - Periodic AARs consolidate learnings into:\r\n 127:   - refined packs,\r\n 128:   - clarified profile specs,\r\n 129:   - updated doctrine (rare).\r\n 130: \r\n 131: ## 6. Tooling Interface Rules\r\n 132: - Cross-language integration is file/CLI-based (schemas, traces, manifests).\r\n 133: - OCaml oracle runs as CLI.\r\n 134: - Lean and TLC run under orchestration; no manual “tribal incantations.”\r\n 135: \r\n 136: ### 6.1 Meta CLI Contract\r\n 137: Canonical command families include:\r\n 138: - `meta check`\r\n 139: - `meta resolve`\r\n 140: - `meta run-pack`\r\n 141: - `meta report`\r\n 142: - `meta pin-profile`\r\n 143: \r\n 144: Each command must emit machine-readable artifacts suitable for CI gating and local replay.\r\n 145: \r\n 146: ### 6.2 Obligation Resolver Semantics\r\n 147: - Resolver inputs include:\r\n 148:   - changed files, profile definitions, pack declarations, capability manifests, previous pack fingerprints.\r\n 149: - Resolver outputs include:\r\n 150:   - impacted obligation closure, execution plan, cache hit/miss report, required version-bump notes.\r\n 151: - Caching must be fingerprinted and deterministic; cache reuse is allowed only on matching fingerprints and schema versions.\r\n 152: \r\n 153: ### 6.3 Local vs CI Modes\r\n 154: - Local mode may skip heavyweight packs for cycle-time, but must still compute full impacted closure.\r\n 155: - CI mode executes full required closure and is the authority for merge readiness.\r\n 156: \r\n 157: ### 6.4 Tooling Language Policy\r\n 158: - Repository tooling implementations are .NET-first (C# or F#) unless explicitly approved otherwise.\r\n 159: - Python is not an allowed tooling implementation language in this repository by default.\r\n 160: - Any Python exception requires an explicit logged approval record, including:\r\n 161:   - scope and owner,\r\n 162:   - rationale for exception,\r\n 163:   - sunset/replacement plan.\r\n 164: - PowerShell (`pwsh`) is permitted for convenience orchestration and launcher scripts, but behavior-critical tool runtime logic (for example empirical Excel driving and artifact emission) must remain in stable .NET tools.\r\n 165: \r\n 166: ## 7. Deliverable Names per Round\r\n 167: - Round 0: **DnaVisiCalc** (Pathfinder) — proves the verification + meta-control loop.\r\n 168: - Round 1: **DnaPreCalc** — first full end-to-end implementation and spec push.\r\n 169: - Round 2: **DnaSuperCalc** — refactor/polish and “too-perfect” exploration.\r\n 170: - Round 3: **DnaCalc** — streamlined, maintainable Goldilocks product.\r\n 171: \r\n 172: ### 7.1 Round 0 Functional Scope Authority\r\n 173: - For DnaVisiCalc pathfinder v0 functional-scope questions, use:\r\n 174:   - `..\\\\DnaVisiCalc\\\\docs\\\\SPEC_v0.md`\r\n 175:   - `..\\\\DnaVisiCalc\\\\docs\\\\ENGINE_REQUIREMENTS.md`\r\n 176:   - `..\\\\DnaVisiCalc\\\\docs\\\\ENGINE_API.md`\r\n 177: - Foundation docs remain the source of truth for doctrine, architecture framing, and operations process, and must stay consistent with that upstream functional contract.\r\n 178: - Proposed functional-scope expansions discovered in implementation (for example from gap-analysis style docs) are tracked as follow-on backlog and routed through synthesis before any doctrine/policy promotion.\r\n 179: \r\n 180: ## 8. Managed-Run Discipline (Prompt, Research, Synthesis, Reference)\r\n 181: Prompt execution, deep research, synthesis, and reference-spec processing are treated as managed operational activities with run artifacts.\r\n 182: \r\n 183: ### 8.0 Managed-Run Task Types\r\n 184: - `prompt_run`: reusable prompt execution runs under `prompts/runs/<run-id>/`.\r\n 185: - `research_run`: deep research runs under `research/runs/<run-id>/`.\r\n 186: - `synthesis_run`: synthesis/decision runs under `synthesis/runs/<run-id>/`.\r\n 187: - `reference_run`: reference-spec processing and conformance-candidate extraction runs under `reference/runs/<run-id>/`.\r\n 188: \r\n 189: ### 8.1 Prompt Runs\r\n 190: - Prompt-run operating procedure lives in `prompts/README.md`.\r\n 191: - Prompt runs must capture raw outputs, manifests, and trace artifacts under `prompts/runs/<run-id>/`.\r\n 192: - Prompt outputs are inputs to decision-making, not source-of-truth policy.\r\n 193: \r\n 194: ### 8.2 Synthesis Runs\r\n 195: - Synthesis-run operating procedure lives in `synthesis/README.md`.\r\n 196: - Synthesis runs must record per-suggestion decisions (`accept` / `adapt` / `defer` / `reject`) with rationale and target-document references.\r\n 197: - No synthesis edit should be applied without a corresponding decision-log record.\r\n 198: - Synthesis artifacts are audit/history records; source-of-truth remains `CHARTER.md`, `ARCHITECTURE_AND_REQUIREMENTS.md`, `OPERATIONS.md`, and `notes/RESEARCH_NOTES.md` for non-doctrinal retained knowledge.\r\n 199: \r\n 200: ### 8.3 Research Runs\r\n 201: - Deep-research prompt templates live in `prompts/PROMPT_PACK_DEEP_RESEARCH.md`.\r\n 202: - Research topic and source registries live under `research/`.\r\n 203: - Research runs must capture exact prompt input text, source links, and output artifacts under `research/runs/<run-id>/`.\r\n 204: - Research outputs are evidence inputs; they do not become doctrine until synthesized into source-of-truth docs.\r\n 205: \r\n 206: ### 8.4 Document Precedence During Synthesis\r\n 207: When synthesis suggestions conflict, precedence remains:\r\n 208: 1. `CHARTER.md`\r\n 209: 2. `ARCHITECTURE_AND_REQUIREMENTS.md`\r\n 210: 3. `OPERATIONS.md`\r\n 211: 4. `notes/RESEARCH_NOTES.md`\r\n 212: 5. `notes/BRAINSTORM_NOTES.md`\r\n 213: \r\n 214: ### 8.5 Synthesis Completion and Status Model\r\n 215: - A synthesis run is complete only when all of the following exist:\r\n 216:   - frozen input hashes,\r\n 217:   - full suggestion index and decision log coverage for the scoped run set,\r\n 218:   - applied/adapted changes reflected in source-of-truth docs,\r\n 219:   - output synthesis report,\r\n 220:   - source run manifests or registries marked `synthesized` with reference to the synthesis run id.\r\n\r\n=== notes/BRAINSTORM_NOTES.md ===\r\n   1: # BRAINSTORM_NOTES.md — DNA Calc Brainstorm Notes (Captured Ideas)\r\n   2: \r\n   3: This document captures additional ideas, potentials, open questions, and lower-level notes from the discussion. It is intentionally broader and less curated than the Charter/Operations/Architecture docs.\r\n   4: \r\n   5: Round 0 note (2026-02-27): DnaVisiCalc pathfinder functional scope is now stabilized in `..\\\\DnaVisiCalc\\\\docs\\\\SPEC_v0.md`, `ENGINE_REQUIREMENTS.md`, and `ENGINE_API.md`. Items here that differ should be treated as historical brainstorming or post-v0 expansion ideas.\r\n   6: \r\n   7: ## A. Core architectural motifs\r\n   8: - “Small toy built like a big system”: strict boundaries, replaceable adapters, protocol-first design.\r\n   9: - Three hard boundaries: OpLog → DocSnapshot → CalcDeltas.\r\n  10: - Epoch/MVCC model:\r\n  11:   - committed vs stabilized epochs,\r\n  12:   - stale/pending cell states,\r\n  13:   - pin/unpin snapshots and GC policy.\r\n  14: - “Meta-epochs” for project stabilization (profiles + packs + conformance artifacts).\r\n  15: - Profiles + feature gates (core vs interop vs UI behavior).\r\n  16: - Clean-room compatibility philosophy: public docs + reproducible observations only.\r\n  17: \r\n  18: ## B. Verification stack and near-formal posture\r\n  19: - Lean: semantics of formula DSL + structural rewrite lemmas; determinism proofs; optional dependency-extraction soundness.\r\n  20: - TLA+: verify concurrency protocol (stale-commit prevention, snapshot consistency, exclusive mutation, eventual stabilization).\r\n  21: - OCaml: CLI oracle + trace runner + shrinking/minimization.\r\n  22: - File-based integration between tools; avoid in-process embedding of Lean/OCaml in v1.\r\n  23: - Admission rule for new “spec languages”: must have a checker + an artifact + CI integration.\r\n  24: \r\n  25: ## C. Development doctrine and “recalc” analogy\r\n  26: - Development behaves like calculation:\r\n  27:   - change → dirty marking → closure → scheduling → stabilization → commit.\r\n  28: - “Auto vs manual” mode for dev pipeline:\r\n  29:   - local can defer heavy packs; CI must stabilize affected profiles.\r\n  30: - Packs as readiness units; Green has veto on stabilization.\r\n  31: - Triads per module: Design + Assurance + Delivery reps.\r\n  32: - Logistics role: reduce cycle time and improve determinism and reporting.\r\n  33: - Regression discipline: every bug becomes a minimized trace fixture.\r\n  34: \r\n  35: ## D. Team structure\r\n  36: - Green: spec/proofs/TLA+/OCaml oracle/cases/packs.\r\n  37: - Red: Rust implementation.\r\n  38: - Blue: .NET implementation.\r\n  39: - Tooling may be .NET-first; Rust utilities may exist for high-perf targeted tools.\r\n  40: \r\n  41: ## E. UI ideas (Tauri/web stack)\r\n  42: - Canvas/WebGL grid renderer; DOM overlay editor for IME and selection.\r\n  43: - Never create DOM-per-cell; use virtualization.\r\n  44: - Tile caching (e.g., 256–512px tiles), dirty rectangles, text measurement caching.\r\n  45: - Explicit view-state reducer/state machine:\r\n  46:   - selecting, editing, formula ref-picking, fill handle, resize, scrolling.\r\n  47: - Geometry/hit-test pure functions; invariants (no gaps/overlaps; hit-test consistent with draw).\r\n  48: - RenderPlan IR for deterministic testing (avoid screenshot brittleness).\r\n  49: - Display staleness explicitly (value_epoch indicators).\r\n  50: \r\n  51: ## F. Calc engine ideas\r\n  52: - Pipeline: parse → bind → dependency graph → incremental recompute → caching.\r\n  53: - Stable IDs for cells/objects to survive structural edits; avoid raw “A1 string keys” internally.\r\n  54: - Structural edits treated as atomic doc transformations (exclusive mutation path).\r\n  55: - Prioritized recompute (viewport-first) as later optimization.\r\n  56: - Determinism vs parallelism:\r\n  57:   - fixed reduction order for SUM to avoid float nondeterminism (policy decision).\r\n  58: - Internal caches must be bounded; avoid logical memory leaks; avoid Arc cycles.\r\n  59: - Coordinator owns mutation; workers are pure computations on immutable snapshots.\r\n  60: \r\n  61: ## G. External inputs, UDFs, XLL, RTD\r\n  62: - STREAM(\"topic\") for deterministic external data in Pathfinder.\r\n  63: - Full RTD support is required in full system (topic lifecycle and update-triggered invalidation).\r\n  64: - External UDF registration:\r\n  65:   - metadata: name/arity/volatility class (`Standard` / `Volatile` / `ExternallyInvalidated`).\r\n  66:   - external invalidation path is distinct from volatile invalidation path.\r\n  67:   - async UDF continuations deferred unless absolutely needed in Pathfinder.\r\n  68: - XLL boundary contracts formally specified; in-process XLL host in full system.\r\n  69: \r\n  70: ## H. VBA and macro world\r\n  71: - VBA is outside core.\r\n  72: - Engine stores VBA project as blob + metadata; app layer wires:\r\n  73:   - file load/save ↔ engine blob,\r\n  74:   - VBA editor/runtime ↔ engine via ops.\r\n  75: - Macro execution in exclusive mutation mode; serialized event stream.\r\n  76: - COM automation facade (Windows-only) possibly built on top of the protocol surface.\r\n  77: - Macro recording considered “easy” relative to macro execution; COM controls integration excluded early.\r\n  78: \r\n  79: ## I. File I/O and interop notes\r\n  80: - File I/O should be adapter-based and outside core.\r\n  81: - Round-trip unknown OOXML parts as opaque attachments.\r\n  82: - “Lowering pipeline” for export: internal model → Excel-safe subset → OOXML, with explicit loss markers if needed.\r\n  83: - DIF suggested as a lightweight historical file format for early experiments (Pathfinder/VisiCalc).\r\n  84: - Clean-room rule implies evidence logs and reproducible observations for Excel behaviors.\r\n  85: \r\n  86: ## J. Collaboration\r\n  87: - Collaboration identified as important feature block; design seam early.\r\n  88: - Prefer server-sequenced op log replication first (deterministic shared order).\r\n  89: - CRDT/OT possible later; structural edits and reference rewriting make it hard.\r\n  90: - Choice: share doc only (clients compute locally) vs share oracle values; RTD complicates this.\r\n  91: - Presence/cursor sharing is separate from semantic sharing.\r\n  92: \r\n  93: ## K. “Agentic coding flywheel” influences\r\n  94: - Tool constellation: small CLIs with durable artifacts (sessions, reports, traces).\r\n  95: - Session capture: every run yields a bundle (diff, packs run, results, rationale).\r\n  96: - SQLite artifact store for searches and triage.\r\n  97: - Advisory “area leasing” for multi-agent work to avoid collisions.\r\n  98: - Inspector dashboards:\r\n  99:   - epoch state viewer, dirty set, in-flight tasks,\r\n 100:   - case explorer, conformance dashboard.\r\n 101: \r\n 102: ## L. Requirements vs architecture framing\r\n 103: - Ladder: Mission → Doctrine → Requirements → Architecture + constraints → Intent/Realization.\r\n 104: - Architecture-independent requirements: black-box behaviors and quality targets.\r\n 105: - Architecture-dependent constraints: enforceable “thou shalt” rules.\r\n 106: - Architecture-anchored intents: become precise only once anchored (e.g., epoch staleness rules).\r\n 107: \r\n 108: ## M. Names and round structure\r\n 109: - System family names:\r\n 110:   - DnaVisiCalc (Round 0 pathfinder),\r\n 111:   - DnaPreCalc (Round 1 full system),\r\n 112:   - DnaSuperCalc (Round 2 perfection/refactor),\r\n 113:   - DnaCalc (Round 3 Goldilocks target).\r\n 114: - Mission language adopted with a light nod to “Mission / Doctrine / Orders.”\r\n 115: - Clean-room positioning explicitly included in mission.\r\n 116: \r\n 117: ## N. Open questions (to keep visible)\r\n 118: - Determinism policy under parallelism (float reduction, scheduling).\r\n 119: - External oracle policy for collaboration (local vs shared).\r\n 120: - Export degradation: cell errors vs metadata/comments vs both.\r\n 121: - Async UDF continuations: needed after Pathfinder or later?\r\n 122: - How much “view state” is document-backed vs session-only.\r\n 123: - Minimum set of Excel semantics to define profiles early (subset selection and measurement).\r\n\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}} {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_55","type":"agent_message","text":"The keyword scan is enough to locate the sections.
